15
People are deeply pessimistic about AI’s impact on
jobs in general, yet relatively few expect their own
job to disappear. The bigger concern is how tasks will
change and who will be able to adapt (Figure 10). Net
sentiment towards AI’s impact on jobs stands at -67%,
the most negative reading on AI attitudes in the survey.
However, only 7% of respondents expect AI to replace
their own job entirely within the next five years. A further

21% expect significant parts of their work to be replaced,
while 26% expect AI mainly to assist and augment what
they do. Another 25% foresee little or no impact, while
20% do not know. The apparent paradox between very
negative views of AI’s overall impact on employment
and relatively limited fears of personal job loss can
therefore be reconciled: people expect AI to reshape
tasks and job content even if they do not expect mass
unemployment (see our forecast on the macroeconomic

and labor market impact of AI in Box 2).
Across countries, the main difference is not whether
people expect change, but whether they see AI primarily
as a substitute, a complement or an unknown. The US
records the highest expectation of outright displacement:
12% expect their job to disappear entirely, almost twice
the European average, and another 21% expect significant
parts to be replaced. Spain also anticipates substantial
change, with 25% expecting significant task replacement,

but this is matched by the highest share expecting AI to
assist their work (31%, alongside Austria) and the lowest
level of uncertainty in the sample at just 13%. Austria
similarly leans towards augmentation, with 31% expecting
AI to assist and support them. France and Germany, by
contrast, stand out less for replacement fears than for
uncertainty: around one in four respondents do not know
how AI will affect their job. These differences suggest
that labor-market anxiety has different forms across

countries: substitution fears are more pronounced in the
US, expectations of task reconfiguration are strongest in
Spain, while parts of Europe remain unsure about what the
transition will mean in practice.

disruption rather than job
destruction

Expectations of both augmentation and automation

due to AI are highest among younger and highly-
educated respondents, the same groups that are also
more exposed to the technology in their daily lives and
work. Some 43% of Gen Z expect AI to replace their job
either entirely or in significant part, compared with just
14% of Boomers. At the same time, 34% of Gen Z expect
AI mainly to assist them at work, more than twice the
share among Boomers (15%), while only 11% say they do
not know what AI will mean for their job, compared with

34% of Boomers. A similar pattern emerges by education.
Almost one-third of tertiary-educated respondents
expect full or significant replacement (32%), compared
with 28% among those with primary education, but
they are also almost three times as likely to expect
augmentation (33% versus 12%) and far less likely to be
uncertain (14% versus 36%). Income differences point
in the same direction: high-income respondents are
somewhat more likely than low-income respondents to

expect full or significant replacement (34% versus 29%),
but also more likely to expect AI to assist them (26% versus
17%), while uncertainty is much higher among low-income
respondents (29% versus 17%). The divide is therefore not
simply between groups that expect disruption and those
that do not: the groups most exposed to AI tend to expect
more change in both directions, while less exposed groups
are much more uncertain about what the technology will

mean for them.

### Page 17

14 October 2026
17
To assess whether direct exposure to AI changes
how people expect it to affect their jobs, we split
respondents into three mutually exclusive groups
according to how they currently use the technology.
Around one-third already use AI at work (33%),
comprising both those who use it mainly at work
and equally at work and at home, while 37% use it
only privately and 30% do not use AI at all. We then
compare expectations for AI’s impact on their own

job across these three groups, using the full sample
as a benchmark. This allows us to distinguish whether
greater exposure is associated primarily with fears of
replacement, expectations of augmentation or simply a
clearer view of how work is likely to change.
The more people use AI at work, the more job
transformation they expect, but exposure raises
expectations of augmentation even more strongly
than fears of replacement (Figure 11). Among AI users

at work, 40% expect AI to replace their job entirely or
take over significant parts of it, compared with just 17%
of non-users. However, the gap is even larger on the
positive side: 41% of workplace users expect AI mainly to
assist and support them, almost four times the 11% share
among non-users. This asymmetry fits with our earlier
finding that people who already use AI tend to view the
technology more positively. Exposure appears to make
both the disruptive and complementary potential of AI

more tangible, but it is especially the expected upside
that is missing among non-users.
The other striking gradient is uncertainty, which falls
sharply as exposure to AI increases. Only 11% expect
augmentation, while 33% foresee little or no impact and
another 39% simply do not know what AI will mean for
their job. Together, almost three-quarters of non-users
therefore either expect no meaningful effect or cannot yet
assess one, compared with just 19% of workplace users.

Private-only users again occupy the middle ground: 27%
expect full or significant replacement, 26% augmentation,
30% little or no impact and 18% remain uncertain. The
gradient suggests that direct experience shifts perceptions
away from indifference and uncertainty towards a
much more concrete view of AI as both a source of task
substitution and a tool for productive support. AI users
have thus a much clearer view of how the technology
could change their work, seeing both greater disruption

and substantially greater potential for augmentation.

Allianz Research
AI is likely to reshape far more jobs than it destroys, but the scale of labor-market adjustment will still be
substantial. Our estimates suggest that AI could affect 23% of jobs across the major advanced economies over the next
one to three years³.  The estimate is built bottom-up from more than 800 occupations, which we classify according to
four possible AI impacts: no exposure, automation, augmentation or reorganization. We aggregate these occupation-

level effects into sectoral AI-exposure scores and then to the country level, taking into account sectoral employment
shares as well as structural factors such as AI adoption and preparedness, labor-market regulation and unit labor
costs. Job reorganization is the largest channel, accounting for 10% of jobs, as workers remain in their roles but AI takes
over or changes some of their tasks. A further 8% of jobs face a higher risk of automation, while 5% could benefit from

augmentation. Exposure across countries differs markedly, largely because of differences in their sectoral composition
as well as structural factors, such as labor market regulations and the institutional context. In the US, where finance,
ICT and other knowledge-intensive services account for a larger share of employment, 29% of jobs could be affected,
compared with 15% across the major European economies. Within Europe, the share ranges from 18% in the UK and 16%

in Germany to 14% in France, 12% in Spain and just 9% in Italy. The US therefore faces greater near-term disruption, but is
also better-positioned to capture the productivity and employment gains from AI.
The first effects are likely to appear in hiring and job composition rather than in a sudden rise in aggregate
unemployment. Early evidence already points in this direction: more AI-intensive sectors in the Eurozone have seen
weaker employment growth since late 2022, while younger and less-experienced white-collar workers in routine

cognitive occupations appear particularly exposed. AI may therefore initially show up through fewer entry-level
positions, slower hiring and weaker wage growth in exposed occupations rather than large-scale layoffs. The main
medium-term risk is a transition gap: firms can reorganize and automate tasks faster than workers can move into newly
created roles. Around 25mn jobs across the US and the major European economies are estimated to be subject to

automation risk, while new AI-related employment will only partly offset displacement in the near and medium term.
Over the longer term, we expect both substantial productivity gains and a moderately negative impact on
employment, but the gains are likely to be larger in the US than in Europe (Figure 12). Our ten-year estimates assume
that the AI transition follows a so-called J-curve, with investments and costs upfront, while the gains will only be realized

later⁴. AI-related investment already adds around 0.4pp to US GDP growth in 2026, but the immediate productivity gains
are limited as they get absorbed by reorganization costs and labor-market adjustment. By 2030, US GDP is projected
to grow  +0.6% faster annually, while employment will be 1.5% lower relative to a world without AI. Thereafter, the
productivity dividend becomes more visible: by 2036, cumulative labor productivity is around +4.8% above the no-AI

baseline in the US, while the employment effect largely unwinds to -0.1%, equivalent to roughly -170,000 jobs. In Europe,
productivity gains are more muted and employment effects more persistent: employment remains below the no-AI
baseline across the major economies, reaching around -2.9% in Spain (-650,000 jobs) and -3.1% in Italy (-740,000 jobs),
while Italy records the smallest productivity gain at around +1.8%. Germany and France also remain below baseline

on employment, although by less than Spain and Italy. However, these employment losses will unfold against a rapidly
shrinking workforce in the ageing European societies: by 2036, labor supply is set to decline by 5.6mn workers in
Germany, 4.2mn in Italy and 2.3mn in Spain. Europe therefore faces a double labor-market challenge from demographic
change and AI. The silver lining is that AI may partly compensate for a shrinking workforce in aggregate, but not

one-for-one, because AI-related displacement will not necessarily occur in the same occupations, sectors and regions
where labor shortages emerge. Overall, the US combines the highest near-term AI exposure with the smallest long-run
employment loss, reflecting faster adoption, stronger investment and more effective reallocation. Europe, by contrast,
faces a more persistent employment drag and smaller productivity gains, but part of the AI-related displacement could

help cushion the effects of ageing.

The preparedness gap is starkest in training:
adaptation is reaching those already ahead faster
than those most in need of it. Among employed

respondents, only 17% have received AI training at
work, while 44% say their employer offers none (Figure
13). Participation reaches 30% among employed
Americans but just 10% among Poles, and is markedly
higher for men than women, at 21% against 14%. The
education gradient is steeper still, with tertiary-educated
employees roughly three times as likely to have received
training as the secondary-educated. The generational
pattern is more nuanced: Millennials, rather than Gen

Z, record the highest training participation, promoter
share and productivity expectations, while Gen Z is more
likely to be offered training but decline it. The result is
a preparedness gap between anticipated disruption
and actual investment in adaptation. Left unchecked,
AI diffusion risks reinforcing existing advantages rather
than broadening opportunity.
Two demographic patterns sharpen that preparedness
challenge. The first is that the gender divide is often one

of uncertainty rather than outright opinion. Across multiple
questions, women are substantially more likely than men
to answer “don’t know”, with the gap reaching 25% versus
14% on AI’s effect on inequality and 28% versus 20% on
whether their own job will change. Women are therefore
not consistently more hostile to AI; they are less certain
about its implications. The second is a recurring U-shape
by financial strain. Both the financially comfortable and

the most strained tend to be more engaged and more
polarized than those in the middle, whether in their overall
attitudes, expected job disruption or willingness to retrain.
Even among respondents who find it very easy to make
ends meet, a majority still see AI as negative for jobs.
Labor-market anxiety is therefore not confined to the
economically vulnerable.
Preparedness: so far training
reaches mainly those already
ahead

### Page 21

14 October 2026
21
Beyond their own jobs, people expect AI to widen the
gap between winners and losers (Figure 14). Across
the sample, 37% expect AI to increase inequality in
their country, against just 9% who expect it to narrow,
for a net of +28%. This concern is shared across all
countries, from Austria (+40%) to Poland (+20%). The
fear is not simply that AI will replace jobs, but that its
benefits will accrue to some while others bear the costs.

Together with widespread concerns about labor-market
disruption, these findings point to a broader anxiety
about who will benefit from the AI revolution. The
challenge is therefore not only to unlock AI‘s productivity
potential but also to ensure that its gains are widely
shared.
For most people, AI‘s biggest risks are not hypothetical
future scenarios but immediate threats to information,
privacy and jobs. Misinformation and information
manipulation top the list of concerns, cited by 25% of

respondents, followed by data privacy and misuse of
personal information (21%) and job losses (17%) (Figure
15). The pattern is remarkably consistent across countries,
from Austria to the US. The loss of human control over
advanced AI systems, a prominent concern in the AI-safety
debate, ranks fourth at 16%, while the concentration of
power among large technology companies is cited by 9%,
with particularly limited concern in the US, home to the
leading AI developers. Only 12% of respondents say they

have no concerns about AI at all. The message is clear:
People are worried less about what AI might become than
about the harm it can already cause.
Inequality, misinformation
and privacy concerns: people’s
worries culminate in a call for
stricter regulation

Faced with these risks, the public is sending

policymakers an increasingly clear message: AI needs
stronger guardrails.  Across the sample, the net regulation
score – the share favoring strict or some regulation minus
those opposing regulation – has risen to 78%, up from
61% in 2025 (Figure 16). Support is strongest in the UK
(85%) and Spain (82%) but exceeds 70% in every surveyed
country, while only around 5% favor no regulation.
Crucially, demand for safeguards is not confined to AI
skeptics: Even respondents who expect AI to improve

their own productivity want stronger oversight. People
appear willing to embrace AI‘s benefits, but not to accept
its risks unchecked. The survey also predates the recent
wave of high-profile AI-related cybersecurity incidents,
which could further heighten public attention to safety
and accountability⁵.
⁵ See: Financial Times (2026), “Hugging Face attack is a wake-up call about the risks of AI” , 3 September 2026.

### Page 24

Allianz Research
24
Governments have taken significantly different
approaches on AI regulation, with the US and the
EU representing contrasting models (Table 1). The
US has no comprehensive federal AI law, relying on a
patchwork of state-level legislation and existing sectoral
rules, alongside federal efforts to limit divergent state
requirements. California has emerged as a leading
state regulator, introducing requirements addressing
transparency, safety and specific uses of AI. The UK

also relies largely on existing sectoral and cross-cutting
legislation, with additional measures under consideration
where gaps emerge. The EU, by contrast, has established
a comprehensive, risk-based framework through its AI
Act. In force since 2024, its obligations are being phased
in through 2028, with member states responsible for much
of the national enforcement framework. Italy has also
introduced supplementary national legislation, including

criminal provisions⁶. Outside the EU, public demand for
regulation thus runs well ahead of the rules in place.
The policy challenge is not simply to regulate
more, but to regulate what matters without stifling
innovation. Too little regulation can leave firms with
weak incentives to account for safety risks and other
harms, while allowing concentration in areas such as
hardware and data to entrench incumbents. Too much
or poorly designed regulation can raise the cost of

experimentation, slow AI adoption and investment and
weigh disproportionately on smaller firms, which are
less able to absorb compliance costs⁷. The objective for
policymakers should therefore be to address harmful
externalities and preserve trust and competition while
keeping barriers to experimentation and diffusion as
low as possible. As public demand for regulation rises,
getting this balance right will become increasingly
important.

### Page 25

14 October 2026
25
For Europe, the central challenge is not simply to accelerate AI adoption, but to turn widespread experimentation
into productive, inclusive and trusted use Curiosity is not the main constraint: Europeans have embraced AI almost as
widely as Americans, but the technology has made fewer inroads into the workplace, and workers are less prepared to
use it effectively. The priority is therefore to remove the barriers that prevent AI from translating into productivity gains

and broader economic benefits. This requires action on five fronts:
1. Move AI from private experimentation into productive workplace use.  General-purpose technologies like
AI generate productivity gains only after firms make large complementary investments. These investments
show up as costs today, dragging down earnings and productivity in the short run, the so-called productivity
J-curve. Because the costs are immediate while the benefits are delayed, private investment may fall short,

in particular for SMEs, which face disproportionately high costs for the same implementation effort⁸. Europe
should reduce these costs that are holding back implementation, especially for SMEs, through accelerated
depreciation or temporary tax credits for identifiable complementary investments such as software integration,
data infrastructure, cybersecurity and process redesign. Public support should be tied to actual deployment
in business processes and, where possible, measurable productivity or efficiency gains rather than generic AI

subscriptions.
2. Close the training gap by starting with employers. This is primarily a provision problem: many workers
receive no offer of AI training, while most of those offered it take it up or plan to do so. Employer-led training
should therefore be supported through training tax credits, public co-financing and individual learning accounts,
with a focus on occupation-specific skills and groups currently least served including women, mid-career workers,

the secondary-educated and employees in smaller firms. Europe already has useful instruments: Germany’s
“Qualifizierungschancengesetz” subsidizes training costs and, in some cases, wage costs for employees affected
by technological change, while the EU  encourages member states to develop individual learning accounts that
give workers portable training entitlements. Where firms receive public support for major AI investments, this
could be linked to a workforce-training plan.

3. Shift labor-market policy from protecting jobs to supporting transitions.  Because AI is likely to change tasks
and occupations more extensively than it eliminates jobs outright, governments need to identify adjustment
pressures before they translate into unemployment. Combining occupation-level AI-exposure indicators
with vacancy, wage and skill-demand data can help identify vulnerable workers and emerging employment
opportunities. Workers in exposed occupations should have access to retraining while still employed, stronger

job-matching services, mobility grants and temporary wage insurance where moving into a new role initially
means lower pay. Such measures must go beyond traditional active labor-market programs, whose short-term
effects are often limited and vary considerably across program types and participants. Better targeting is crucial:
Policy priorities:
adopting AI better, not just faster
⁸  For productivity discussion, see: Brynjolfsson, Rock & Syverson 2021. „The Productivity J-Curve: How Intangibles Complement General Purpose

Technologies,“ American Economic Journal: Macroeconomics. For SMEs discussion, see: OECD 2025, “ AI adoption by small and medium-sized enter -
prises”,

### Page 26

Allianz Research
26
programs that invest in human capital accumulation, such as classroom-based vocational training and on-the-
job traineeships, show large gains, in particular for the long-term unemployed, whose skills have eroded during
extended periods out of work, and for women⁹.  Recent evidence confirms that well-designed interventions
do indeed deliver: cost-benefit analyses show that such training schemes can be highly cost effective, and

programs combining part-time work placements with training and short-term community work placements for
the unemployed produce lasting earnings gains for participants¹⁰. The key lesson is therefore to replace broad,
expensive programs with measures that are tightly linked to real-time labor-market signals.
4. Make sure the productivity dividend is broadly shared. AI is likely to raise productivity but could also
shift national income from labor to capital, and if automation outruns capital accumulation, wages could fall

even while output rises¹¹. Policy should therefore address both labor income and household wealth. On the
income side, broader access to skills can help translate productivity gains into better employment prospects
and wages. . On the asset side, funded pensions, employee share plans and wider household capital-market
participation can help more people benefit directly from AI-driven returns, particularly in Europe, where  equity
ownership is heavily concentrated among wealthier households¹². At the same time, the tax system should

avoid unnecessarily favoring capital over labor and adapt if a larger share of income shifts toward capital and
economic rents. Europe is particularly exposed here, as its tax systems rely more heavily on labor income taxes
and social contributions than the US, potentially putting greater pressure on public finances if the labor share of
income declines¹³.
5. Strengthen trust by targeting the risks people actually identify. Misinformation, data misuse and labor-

and human oversight and appeal rights for high-impact AI decisions. Rules should be credible and proportionate:
safeguards that build trust can support adoption, while complex or inconsistent requirements can slow diffusion,
especially for smaller firms. EU-wide templates, sector-specific guidance and regulatory sandboxes can help
make implementation more predictable without weakening protections.

The survey queried more than 8,000 respondents across eight countries: Austria, France, Germany, Italy, Poland, Spain,
the United Kingdom and the United States. It was conducted as a web-based survey between 22 April and 25 May 2026.
Overall responsibility lies with Allianz Research, Allianz Investment Management SE. Sample planning and fieldwork

were carried out by Qualtrics.
Respondents were selected using representative quota sampling. Qualtrics was given quotas specifying how many
people to survey in each country and which selection criteria to apply, with quotas set in line with official statistics for
gender and age groups. A comparison with official statistics confirms that the resulting sample broadly corresponds to
the population aged 18 and over in each of the eight countries.

## Document 247: Australian Business Conditions and Confidence, September (2026-10-20)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Australian Business Conditions and Confidence, September (2026-10-20).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-10-20
Pages: 12
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

#businessconditions #confidence #aud #australia
Australian Business Conditions and Confidence, September
By Luka Belobrajdic
Economist, Westpac
14:15 October 13 2026 Share
Confidence posts its sharpest fall since the start of the Middle East conflict as elevated fuel
prices and higher interest rates weigh on business sentiment, while conditions hold steady
at weak levels. Conditions unchanged at -1 / Confidence -4pts to -13.

### Page 2

The September NAB Business Survey was in the field from 23 to 30 September, capturing ongoing
global uncertainty, still-rising fuel costs and, at least in part, the RBA’s September rate hike. Business
confidence continued to deteriorate, recording its sharpest fall since March and the onset of the Middle
East conflict. The index declined 4pts to -13 and, while remaining above the lows recorded earlier this
year, is now at its weakest level since May. At 18pts below its long-run average, the result underscores

the deep pessimism that continues to weigh on businesses. This broadly mirrors the pessimism evident
in the Westpac-Melbourne Institute Consumer Sentiment Index, where elevated fuel prices and higher
interest rates have coalesced in a similarly weak assessment of the outlook.
The headline business conditions index held steady in negative territory at -1, albeit rising 0.25pts on
an unrounded basis. While encouraging that conditions have not deteriorated further, the index has

now remained at -1 (rounded) for two consecutive months, its weakest level since the height of the
COVID-19 pandemic in 2020. At 7pts below its long-run average, the result points to continued
pressure across the business sector.
Across the major sub-components, employment moved into negative territory, falling 3pts to -1 and
sitting 4pts below its long-run average. This marks the first outright negative reading since November
2020 and provides further evidence of the gradual easing in labour market conditions. Against the

backdrop of compressed margins, the result may also indicate firms are becoming increasingly
cautious around hiring decisions. By contrast, trading conditions rose 1pt to 4, although they remain
8pts below their long-run average. The relative resilience in trading conditions is broadly consistent with

### Page 3

Westpac DataX Card-Tracker data, which suggests nominal spending remained resilient, albeit
slowing, through the September quarter despite ongoing headwinds facing consumers. Profitability
recorded the strongest improvement across the major components, rising 3pts to -5, although the sub-
index remains deeply subdued at 10pts below its long-run average.
Capacity utilisation edged 0.2ppt lower to 82.3%. While the monthly decline provides a tentative signal

of easing capacity pressures, utilisation remains 0.9ppt above its long-run average and continues to
trend higher on a three-month average basis. Capital expenditure continued to recover from its mid-
year weakness, rising 3pts to 7. While still below the highs recorded in the first quarter, the sub-index is
now trending higher on a three-month average basis, pointing to some stabilisation in investment
intentions.
Cost growth was comparatively subdued in September, although purchase cost pressures remain

elevated. Purchase cost growth edged 0.1ppt higher to 2.4%qtr, sitting 1.1ppt above its long-run
average and continuing to weigh on margins. By contrast, labour cost growth eased 0.1ppt to 1.7%qtr,
although it remains 0.5ppt above its long-run average. Final product price growth was unchanged at
0.8%qtr, 0.2ppt above its long-run average. The widening gap between purchase cost and final product
price growth suggests firms continue to absorb a meaningful share of higher input costs, consistent

with profitability remaining deeply below average. Looking ahead, the October survey will provide an
early indication of how the card surcharging ban is affecting business costs and the extent to which
these are passed through to final prices.

### Page 4

Related articles
Among other indicators, forward orders declined 1pt to -1, pointing to continued caution around the
demand outlook. Inventories, by contrast, rose 4pts (rounded) to 7, some 5pts above their long-run
average. In combination with weak forward orders, the inventory build points to a degree of slack
emerging across the business sector.

ANALYST: Tajinder Dhillon, CFA October 1, 2026 
tajinder.dhillon@lseg.com; 
THIS WEEK IN EARNINGS 
AGGREGATE ESTIMATES AND REVISIONS 
 26Q3 Y/Y earnings are expected to be 30.6%. Excluding the energy sector, the Y/Y earnings estimate is 26.8%. 
 Of the 13 companies in the S&P 500 that have reported earnings to date for 26Q3, 84.6% have reported earnings 
above analyst estimates. This compares to a long-term average of 67.7% and prior four quarter average of 81.5%. 
 26Q3 Y/Y revenue is expected to be 11.6%. Excluding the energy sector, the growth estimate is 11.2%. 
 76.9% of companies have reported 26Q3 revenue above analyst expectations. This compares to a long-term 
average of 63% and an average over the past four quarters of 76.8%. 
 For 26Q3, there have been 40 negative EPS preannouncements issued by S&P 500 corporations compared to 65 
positive EPS preannouncements. By dividing 40 by 65 the N/P ratio is 0.6 for the S&P 500 Index.  
 The forward four-quarter (26Q4– 27Q3) P/E ratio for the S&P 500 is 20.1. 
 During the week of Oct. 5, three S&P 500 companies are expected to report quarterly earnings.

There has been an increase in the share-weighted earnings for the S&P 500 since the start of the quarter (to $773.2B 
from $760.7B). Seven of the eleven sectors have experienced downward revisions to estimates.

Since Aug. 1, the materials (-9.1%) and health care (-2.2%) sectors have recorded the highest percentage decreases 
in earnings, while the energy (16.3%) sector has recorded the highest percentage increase in earnings. Overall, share-
weighted earnings for the S&P 500 have increased by 1.6% since the start of the quarter.

Since Aug. 1, the health care (-1.7B) and materials (-1.5B) sectors have recorded the highest dollar-level decreases in 
earnings, while the information technology (8.3B) sector has recorded the highest dollar-level increase in earnings. 
Overall, expected share-weighted earnings for the S&P 500 have increased by 12.5B since the start of the quarter.

earnings above analyst expectations and 7.7% reported earnings below analyst expectations. In a typical quarter 
(since 1994), 67% of companies beat estimates and 20% miss estimates. Over the past four quarters, 82% of 
companies beat the estimates and 15% missed estimates. 
In aggregate, companies are reporting earnings that are 5.3% above estimates, which compares to a long-term (since 
1994) average surprise factor of 4.4% and the average surprise factor over the prior four quarters of 7.7%.

The estimated earnings growth rate for the S&P 500 for 26Q3 is 30.6%. If the energy sector is excluded, the growth 
rate declines to 26.8%. The S&P 500 expects to see share-weighted earnings of $773.2B in 26Q3, compared to share-
weighted earnings of $591.9B (based on the year-ago earnings of the current 504 constituents) in 25Q3. 
11 of the 11 sectors in the index expect to see an improvement in earnings relative to 25Q3. The Energy and 
Information Technology sectors have the highest earnings growth rates for the quarter, while the Consumer Staples 
sector has the weakest anticipated growth compared to 25Q3. 
The Energy sector has the highest earnings growth rate (114.7%) of any sector. It is expected to earn $55.1B in 26Q3, 
compared to earnings of $25.7B in 25Q3. 4 of the 5 sub-industries in the sector are anticipated to see higher earnings 
than a year ago. The Oil & Gas Refining & Marketing (445.6%) and Integrated Oil & Gas (116.1%) sub-industries have 
the highest earnings growth in the sector. If these sub-industries are removed, the growth rate declines to 25.1%. 
The Information Technology sector has the second highest earnings growth rate (66.5%) of any sector. It is expected 
to earn $256.1B in 26Q3, compared to earnings of $153.8B in 25Q3. 12 of the 12 sub-industries in the sector are 
anticipated to see higher earnings than a year ago. The Semiconductors (135.6%) and Semiconductor Materials & 
Equipment (68.9%) sub-industries have the highest earnings growth in the sector. If these sub-industries are removed, 
the growth rate declines to 25.3%. 
The Consumer Staples sector has the lowest earnings growth rate (3.2%) of any sector. It is expected to earn $33.7B 
in 26Q3, compared to earnings of $32.6B in 25Q3. 3 of the 10 sub-industries in the sector are anticipated to see lower 
earnings than a year ago. The Packaged Foods & Meats (-6.5%) and Household Products (-4.2%) sub-industries have 
the lowest earnings growth in the sector. If these sub-industries are removed, the growth rate improves to 6.4%.

STARMINE EARNINGS SURPRISE FORECAST
Looking forward at quarterly performance, we use StarMine’s SmartEstimate® from LSEG to determine which
companies in the S&P 500 are better poised to beat and miss earnings estimates. The SmartEstimate® is a weighted

average of analyst estimates, with more weight given to more recent estimates and more accurate analysts. Our
studies have shown that when the SmartEstimate® differs significantly from the consensus (I/B/E/S mean estimate),
the Predicted Surprise accurately predicts the direction of earnings surprises or further revisions 70% of the time.
When a significant Predicted Surprise for revenue is also present for the period, the accuracy improves to 78%.

StarMine ARM from LSEG is an analyst revisions stock ranking model, designed to predict future changes in analyst
sentiment. ARM incorporates more accurate earnings estimates through the SmartEstimate prediction service. ARM
region rankings scores companies by region on a scale of 1 to 100 where 100 represents the most bullish sentiment.
Over the next two weeks, 31 S&P 500 companies are expected to report earnings. Of these companies, three positive

surprises and five negative surprises are expected from S&P 500 companies reporting quarterly results.

The scenario, forecasts and nowcasts of the Economic Research

### Page 2

UNITED STATES 
The US economy is expected to grow above its potential in 2026, with an average annual growth rate of 2. 2%, close to the 2025 rate (2.1%) . 
This apparent resilience to the uncertainty, tariff and energy shocks, together with above-trend productivity growth, masks K-shaped growth, 
driven by investment linked to AI-optimism and consumption by the wealthiest amid a wealth effect (historically high stock market valuations). 
Inflation overshooting is set to continue (3.4% in 2026) at least through 2028, largely because of the rise in oil prices and tariffs – although the 
impact of these latter appears to be less significant than expected. The labour market is showing clear signs of improvement that should bring 
the unemployment rate down toward 4.0%. Given this shift in price and employment risks amid dynamic growth, the FOMC proceeded with a 
first rate hike at September’s meeting and we expect two further 25bp increases in December 2026 and January 2027, bringing the Fed Funds 
target range at 4,25% - 4,50%.

CHINA 
Economic growth slowed to 4.3% y/y in Q2 2026 vs. 5.0% in Q1. It is projected to reach 4.6% in 2026 as a whole, down from 5% in 2025. Growth 
remains characterized by a K-shaped trajectory, and the gap between the strong performance of exports and the fragility of sectors that rely 
on domestic demand  has even widened this year . Domestic demand has weakened significantly in recent months . Retail sales have barely 
increased, the crisis in the property sector has continued and household confidence remains low. Fiscal policy measures aimed at supporting 
economic growth are expected to increase in the second half of the year. CPI inflation has rebounded slightly since last March, mostly due to 
the rise in global energy prices, as well as due to the increase in electronic good prices and anti-involution measures implemented by the 
authorities. Deflationary pressures persist, however, given weakness in domestic demand.

EUROZONE 
Eurozone growth is expected to slow to 1.1% in 2026 (from 1.3% in 2025), before strengthening to 1.5% in 2027, supported by higher defence 
and infrastructure spending as well as the global AI capex cycle. The economy has proven resilient to the energy shock, notab ly thanks to the 
increasingly tangible impact of G erman defence spending on the real economy. Elevated energy prices in the autumn (especially for natural 
gas), combined with resilient growth, are set to accelerate inflation to a peak around the turn of the year. Annual average i nflation is thus 
expected to reach  3.0% in 2026 (from 2.1% in 2025), before easing to 2.8% in 2027. Given persistent inflation and resilient growth, we 
anticipate another 25bp hike from the ECB in December 2026, bringing the deposit rate to 2.75%, followed by an extended pause . That said, 
stronger than expected second-round effects could lead to more tightening than our current baseline.

### Page 3

FRANCE 
GDP growth remained low in Q2 (0% after -0.2% in Q1) as a result of deteriorated agricultural and construction output, as well as higher 
inflation (2.4% in 2026 according to our forecast, after 0.9% in 2025) . In 2026, GDP growth should  reach 0.5% (after 0.9% in 2025), driven 
mainly by exports.

UNITED KINGDOM 
UK GDP growth would ease slightly to 1.2% in 2026 (1.3% in 2025), before edging back to 1.3% in 2027. The economy has so far weathered 
both the energy price shock and domestic political volatility. That said, growth is expected to decelerate in the second half  of the year, while 
inflation is set to remain stubbornly above target (averaging 3.2% in both 2026 and 2027), driven by higher energy prices and mounting second-
round effects. In response, we expect the BoE to deliver a single insurance hike in November 2026, bringing the Bank Rate to 4.00%. The BoE 
is then expected to resume easing in H2 2027, with two cuts taking the Bank Rate to 3.50% by end-year.

JAPAN 
Following 1.2% in 2025, Japan's GDP growth is expected to decelerate to 1.0% in 2026, before rebounding to 1.2% in 2027. Higher inflation 
and production costs associated with the energy shock are expected to weigh on the economy’s overall performance. On the other hand, the 
latter is supported by fiscal policy  and AI-related investment. Inflation has generally overshot the 2% y/y target since 2022. Accordingly, the 
Bank of Japan initiated a cautious process of “adjustment in the degree of monetary accommodation” in 2024, lifting the policy rate to 1.25% 
so far (previously negative) – the highest since 1995. We expect two more hikes by the end of Q1 2027, followed by a return to a more cautious 
pace until the terminal rate of +2.5% is reached in H2 2028. Japan is facing long-term rates pressure, illustrated by historically high 10- and 30-
year yields, probably fueled by the level of public debt and the pace of monetary adjustment.

EXCHANGE RATES 
We expect Fed hikes to temporarily offset structural headwinds and support the dollar. EUR/USD would target 1.14 by end -2026, before the 
dollar's structural downtrend resumes toward 1.20 by end-2027, although political and fiscal risk may weigh on the euro next year. The USD/JPY 
appreciation would continue but at a more moderate pace, with the BoJ still behind the curve despite its hikes. By contrast, GBP/USD would 
see relative support underpinned by a modest fiscal risk premium under the new Labour government, and resilient growth.

## Document 410: ‘Cost-Plus’ Costs (2026-10-20)

Source PDF: all report pdf/‘Cost-Plus’ Costs (2026-10-20).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-10-20
Pages: 11
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

#australia #luciellis #labour #rba #productivity
‘Cost-Plus’ Costs
By Luci Ellis
Chief Economist Westpac Group, Westpac
10:40 October 16 2026 Share
Entrenched expectations that cost increases can and should be passed on are precisely
what tight monetary policy is leaning against, but only indirectly.
The recent tendency of some firms to bundle into prices card-acceptance costs that had previously
been surcharged – and then some, in some cases – demonstrates a ‘cost-plus’ approach to pricing.

### Page 2

The recent banning of card surcharges has caused considerable upset this month, even though it is a
reversion to historical and international practice. The card schemes used to impose “no-surcharge”
rules, until the RBA banned these in 2003, and many other countries still have them. The turnaround
represents the RBA throwing in the towel on its longstanding effort to use price incentives to get people
to choose low-cost payment methods. Surcharges no longer made sense in a largely cashless
economy. In any case, least-cost routing puts the power to choose lower-cost methods in the hands of
the merchants who actually bear the cost.

In most cases, firms will simply roll the surcharge into the sticker price, leaving the total cost to the
consumer unchanged, just more transparent. We also understand that some large retailers have
absorbed the cost – if they were surcharging in the first place, which many did not. (The RBA estimated
that around 16% of businesses imposed surcharges.) Inevitably, though, some firms have lifted their
prices by more than any reasonable estimate of the merchant service fee that the surcharge was
meant to cover. And because this is Australia, of course takeaway coffee has been the centre of
consumer discontent.

Higher prices for one’s caffeine fix was unlikely to be the main instigator of this month’s slump in
consumer sentiment. Petrol prices are typically more salient for cost-of-living concerns, and the before-
and-after picture on the RBA announcement that week shows that higher interest rates also contributed
to the decline.

In some senses, though, the post-surcharge prices and the RBA’s decisions are linked: businesses
passing on increased costs into their own prices – and expecting to be able to make that increase stick
– is exactly the kind of inflationary environment the RBA is leaning against. Firms have been subjected
to an array of non-labour cost escalations, from pandemic-era supply chain issues to electricity to fuel
to water to building supplies. And IT equipment prices, which for decades were a reliable source of cost
reductions, are nowadays flat to up. Passing these costs on, leaving margins intact, demonstrates that
demand is not weak enough to prevent this, and signals that firms’ inflationary expectations might be
higher than the RBA desires. The RBA will take this as a sign that demand is still outstripping supply.

To the extent they can pass on these costs through to prices, it suggests that demand was strong
enough to bear it. The RBA will take this as a signal that demand is still outstripping supply.
This is mostly a story about non-labour costs. Base wages growth has been moderate for some
years, though the above-average increase in award wages this year will add a little. Not all firms will
seek to pass these cost increases on, but some will, including because they have limited scope to
absorb the cost increase into margins.
A cost-plus mentality has drawbacks, including that it limits the imagination away from finding
alternative, efficiency-improving responses. It also accords with recent academic research
suggesting that firms form expectations and set prices according to what is happening in their own
industry and experience, while putting little weight on economy-wide inflation or central bank
credibility. This is an unpleasant message for many consumers or anyone in an interest-sensitive
sector when supply shocks abound.

### Page 3

This is mostly a story about non-labour costs. While the Fair Work Commission did hand down an
above-average increase in award wages this year and this was probably passed through to prices in
hospitality and other labour-intensive sectors, base wages growth has been moderate for some years.
Business surveys point to non-labour cost escalation being the real issue lately. This is why we were
puzzled by the passage in the RBA minutes this week suggesting that cost pressures reported in
business surveys might imply that the unemployment rate consistent with inflation at target (the NAIRU)
has risen. It is a convenient belief, though, if unemployment is starting to overshoot your forecasts, as
the latest labour market data have done compared with the RBA’s August forecasts.

Not all firms can pass on cost increases. Most small firms do not have the market power, though coffee
may be an exception because convenience induces a kind of local market power. And as noted above,
large firms with everyday-low-price strategies will instead try to find savings elsewhere in the supply
chain.

Still, some firms will seek to pass these cost increases on. Some observers might see this as corporate
greed, but another way to frame this strategy is simply as a ‘cost-plus’ mentality. It turns out that cost-
plus pricing is especially common in areas where regulators determine the price – water and other
utilities being examples where costs and prices have escalated a lot in recent years.

There are limits to firms’ ability to absorb costs, too. The failures of so many building firms a few years
ago, squeezed between post-pandemic cost escalation, delivery delays and fixed-price contracts, were
a case in point. Normally, there is a contractual response, and it need not end in insolvency. The
episode does illustrate, however, that it is unrealistic to expect firms never to pass cost increases
through to prices.

The cost-plus mentality does have its drawbacks. It limits the imagination away from finding responses
that might save costs elsewhere to absorb the cost hike, and in so doing, boost efficiency. It was
equally limiting in times of soft demand: in the years before the pandemic, many firms professed a
belief that, because they couldn’t get price increases to stick, they could not possibly accept cost
increases, including in wages. Thus, Australia ended up with wages growth too low to be consistent
with inflation at target, and the peculiar spectacle of a central bank governor complaining that wages
growth was too low. And if labour remains cheap, why invest to economise on it and thereby boost
productivity?

A cost-plus mentality also amplifies the pass-through of supply shocks. This is not quite the same thing
as the ‘inflation expectations’ seen in standard economic models of inflation, but it is related. The
stronger the tendency towards cost-plus pricing, the worse the inflation response to a supply shock.
This is also true when inflation expectations are high, but the distinction matters. The recent academic
literature includes some fascinating empirical evidence from New Zealand that suggests that beliefs
about current and future overall inflation have little bearing on many firms’ pricing decisions. As related
work using French data shows, firms’ pricing choices are shaped less by economy-wide inflation
developments than by what is happening to their costs and in their industry.

### Page 4

Related articles
That adds up to economy-wide behaviour that looks a lot like firms (in aggregate) setting prices
according to expected inflation (in aggregate), but under the hood something quite different is going on.
If firms’ beliefs about future economy-wide inflation do not matter much in their pricing decisions, then
neither does the credibility of a central bank’s commitment to return inflation to target. The mechanism
to get inflation down then must be simply to weaken demand enough to convince firms that cost-plus
will not fly in the market. This is an unpleasant message for many consumers or anyone in an interest-
sensitive sector when supply shocks abound. It does, however, imply that if supply shocks fade or even
self-correct, so will high inflation. If and when that happens, tight monetary policy can be unwound.

The deeper question is whether cost-plus price dynamics can be reduced. That is more likely to be a
question for regulators than for central banks.

## Document 421: 【國泰證期研究部】國防軍工產業-無人機、無人艇兵分二路，無人載具供應鏈蓄勢-20261007

Source PDF: all report pdf/【國泰證期研究部】國防軍工產業-無人機、無人艇兵分二路，無人載具供應鏈蓄勢-20261007.pdf

### Page 1

1 
產業更新 – 國防軍工產業 
無人機、無人艇兵分二路，無人載具供應鏈蓄勢 
What’s New –  國防預算持續提高，六年編列 2,400 億元 
2026年國防總支出規劃達 9,495億元，2027年行政院總預算案進一步提高至 1兆 1,225億
元，年增約 18%，後續待立法院審議。 《強化國防自主暨無人載具產業發展條例》規劃六年編
列 2,400 億元，每年以 400 億元為原則，支持可信任供應鏈、研發及測試能力建置。此外，
行政院提出 1,457億元國防追加預算，其中濱海監偵型、濱海攻擊型無人機及小型自殺無人艇
合計 559億元，盼於預算通過後加快採購，於 2027年形成初始戰力。隨政策需求逐步落實，
國內軍工供應鏈將由樣機開發與測試，進一步朝產線建置、零組件穩定供貨及批量交付發展。 
Trends/Outlook – 無人載具供應鏈蓄勢，持續性發展可期 
台灣無人機需求將沿低成本大量部署與高階情監偵兩條路線發展。前者重視採購成本、生產速
度與快速補充能力，帶動模組化設計及共用零組件需求；後者強調長航時、感測品質與穩定資
料鏈，商機涵蓋機體、光電酬載、導控系統及後勤服務。反無人機系統則由單一干擾設備走向
多感測器、指揮控制與反制手段整合，跨品牌相容性、場域驗證及持續更新能力將成為競爭重
點。無人艇方面，產業價值由船體製造延伸至自主航行、通信及任務酬載，台海風浪環境亦提
高海試與可靠度驗證的重要性。研究部認為，隨部署規模擴大，具備系統整合、穩定交付及長
期維保能力的業者，有機會建立持續性收入來源。

Investment Summary 
1. 長榮航太(2645 TT)以軍用商規艦載監偵無人機切入市場，延伸航太製造與品質管理優勢；
漢翔(2634 TT)與 Shield AI合作，以 V-BAT垂直起降無人機取得海巡案，後續可望拓展
在地整合、維保及新增採購需求。創泓科技 (7714 TT )布局 C2 無人機防禦系統與
Sentrycs 反無人機方案，商機涵蓋設備供應、偵測反制及指揮控制整合，後續關注標案
導入與服務收入成長。 
2. 台船(2208 TT )的「奮進魔鬼魚」採三體船與模組化酬載設計，並透過與 Havoc 合作布
局自主水面載具；龍德造船(6753 TT )的「黑潮」以高速操縱與匿蹤設計為特色，結合既
有軍用小型船建造經驗，爭取量產與後勤需求。研究部對台灣軍工產業中期發展持正面
看法，後續隨預算、採購及產品驗證逐步落實，相關業者有望將技術與製造能力轉化為
實質營運成長。

從預算增加到產線擴張，台灣軍工迎來新一輪成長 
過去十年，台灣國防支出的成長，逐步為軍工產業累積更大的發展空間。如今隨著無人機、無人艇
及反制系統成為國防重點，這股需求正從大型軍備採購，延伸至電子、航太與造船供應鏈。以廣義
國防總支出觀察，2026 年原規劃規模達 9,495 億元，若將其分為年度預算與特別預算兩類，年度
預算含基金約 7,627 億元，特別預算約 1,868 億元。年度預算支撐人員、裝備維持與持續性軍事投
資，特別預算則加快重點裝備籌獲，兩者共同擴大產業需求。行政院先前也通過的 2027 年總預算
案，進一步將廣義國防總支出推升至 1 兆 1,225 億元，較 2026 年原規劃增加約 18%，目前送至
立法院審議，仍待後續觀察。 
表一：Taiwan defense budget from 2017 to 2026 
資料來源：行政院主計處、國防部、國泰證期研究部整理

六年 2,400 億元，行政院再提無人載具追加預算 
如果說國防預算擴張為產業打開市場，無人載具專法則讓企業看見更長期的投入方向。9 月行政院
副署的《強化國防自主暨無人載具產業發展條例》 ，要求循年度預算程序，連續六年編列 2,400 億
元，每年以 400 億元為原則，必要時可依需求增編。政策範圍涵蓋可信任供應鏈、研發與測試場
域，也將資源引導至具實質技術及製造能力的業者，讓國防採購同時成為產業升級的推力。此外，
行政院也提出的 1,457 億元國防追加預算，其中「濱海監偵型」、「濱海攻擊型無人機」及「小型自
殺無人艇」三項合計 559 億元。國防部已完成招商說明，希望在預算通過後加快採購，於 2027 年
形成初始戰力。換句話說，產業正走到從樣機、測試走向批量交付的關鍵階段：無人機廠需要準備
產線，零組件供應商需要建立穩定供貨能力，系統整合商則必須完成通信、控制與任務酬載的驗證。
後續追加案等待立法院三讀。

3 
國內無人機需求轉向大量部署及偵察視野延伸 
烏克蘭讓產業看見小型無人機也能改變軍事裝備的需求結構。當無人機從少數專業單位的裝備，成
為前線頻繁使用、需要持續補充的工具，產業競爭也開始從單架性能，延伸至生產速度、成本控制
與快速改版。台灣正沿著這條路徑前進：政府已透過前幾年軍用商規無人機採購累積量產經驗， 9 
月經濟部亦公布中光電相關平台已完成軍用商規量產交付，顯示國內供應鏈逐步跨過展示與驗證階
段。未來市場將形成兩條並行的成長路線。 
1. 低成本、大量部署型無人機，重點在可負擔的採購成本、穩定補充能力與持續更新；烏克蘭常見
的 FPV(第一人稱視角)機型，即展現這類需求的規模潛力。台灣的濱海任務還需要兼顧飛行距離、
海島環境與可信任供應鏈，因此，共用零組件、模組化設計及多家供應，將是逐步降低成本的重要
方向。 
2. 高階情監偵無人機，透過更長滯空時間、更高品質的感測器與穩定資料鏈，擴大海域觀測範圍。
如漢翔已確認與 Shield AI 合作取得海巡署 V-BAT 艦載無人機案，該平台具垂直起降與超過 12 小
時續航能力，有利在有限甲板空間下執行長時間監偵。海軍公開規劃亦包括  2026~2029 年以約 
167.9 億元籌購 32 架銳鳶二及 8 套導控站，另以 360 億元籌購 280 架艦載智慧垂直起降無人機及 
140 套導控系統。這類跨年度計畫帶來的不僅是機體製造，還涵蓋光電酬載、地面站、訓練與後勤
服務；後續採購與在地分工逐步落實後，具備整合及長期維保能力的廠商，有機會取得更高附加價
值。

圖一：Ukrainian FPV Drone - General Cherry 
資料來源：烏克蘭國防部、國泰證期研究部整理

當前線需要更多「看得見」的能力，後方的基地、機場與重要設施，也需要更完整的低空防護能力。
烏克蘭國防部先前就曾指出，Mavic、Autel、Matrice 等多旋翼系統仍持續大量採購，用於前線觀
測，並同步測試替代產品，也說明小型監偵與大型長航時平台各有用途。對台灣而言，這種任務分
層將帶動不同等級的飛控、射頻、光學與電池管理需求，也讓資安、韌體更新及檢測認證成為供應
鏈的重要環節。 
另一方面，反無人機系統正從單一干擾器，走向涵蓋偵測、辨識、指揮與處置的完整架構：雷達與
射頻感測發現目標，光電及熱影像協助確認，指揮控制平台整合資訊，再搭配適合的反制手段。北
約 2026 年公布的反無人機框架採購，同樣採取多感測器與統一指管架構，顯示市場對整合能力的
重視。國內創泓科技已推出 C2 無人機防禦系統，整合訊號偵測、光學辨識及反制模組，9 月進一
步介紹 Sentrycs 代理及在地合作布局，商業機會可由設備供應延伸至系統整合、技術支援與持續
更新。隨光纖操控與自主飛行等技術發展，單靠切斷無線通信的反制方式，也需要更多感測器與處

### Page 4

投資 顧問 
產業報告 
 2026/10/07

4 
置方案配合。研究部認為，反無人機市場的競爭核心，將逐步轉向跨品牌整合、現場驗證及軟體更
新能力；能在複雜環境中降低誤警、穩定辨識目標並持續維護系統的業者，更有機會建立長期客戶
關係。後續觀察國防及關鍵基礎設施標案的導入進度。 
圖二：Ukrainian Small ISR Drone - Mavic 3 Pro 
資料來源：烏克蘭國防部、國泰證期研究部整理

無人艇從船體製造延伸至自主航行與海洋感測 
當船艇能夠在無人乘載的情況下完成巡邏、觀測與運補，造船產業的競爭範圍，也從船體與推進系
統延伸至自主航行、通信與感測器整合。水面無人艇 (USV)可執行海域監偵、通信中繼、搜救、設
施巡檢及軍事任務；水下無人載具 (UUV)則將觀測範圍帶到海面之下，應用於海床測繪、海纜與管
線檢查、反水雷及水下環境偵測。台灣既有的軍艦、商船與高速小型船經驗，也為這個新市場提供
製造基礎。台船「奮進魔鬼魚」船長 8.6 公尺、寬 3.7 公尺，最高航速超過 35 節，採三體船與模
組化酬載設計，並於 2025 年取得 ABS 原則性設計認可(AIP)，為後續產品驗證建立基礎。9 月台
船進一步與 Havoc 宣布合作，擬推動自主水面載具授權共同生產，展現國內造船能力與國際自主
控制技術結合的方向。龍德造船則以「黑潮」切入市場，船長 9 公尺、寬 2 公尺、最高航速超過 
40 節，在 25 節巡航條件下航程約 250 公里，並具自主航行及目標識別功能。隨產品逐步成形，
後續量產採購與任務驗證將成為主要觀察重點。水面平台有望先帶動船體、航控、資料鏈與任務模
組需求，水下市場則進一步考驗聲學設備、耐壓密封、能源及回收能力。 
圖三：台船-奮進魔鬼魚(Endeavor Manta USV) 
資料來源：台船、國泰證期研究部整理

另一方面，台海海象則是無人載具商業化不可忽略的限制。交通部觀測數據顯示，臺中港測站冬季
平均波高約 2.1 公尺，比夏季高約 1.3 公尺，足以說明不同季節的差異性。對小型無人艇，波浪會
增加船體拍擊與姿態變化，影響感測器穩定、推進效率、航速及回收；水下航行雖可減少直接受表
面波浪影響，出入水、母船收放及浮出通信仍受海況約束。對無人機而言，浪高主要透過甲板運動

### Page 5

投資 顧問 
產業報告 
 2026/10/07

5 
與海面飛濺影響艦載起降，飛行本身則更直接受到陣風、降雨及鹽霧影響。因此，廠商需要驗證的
不只是最高航速，還包括不同波高、週期、浪向及酬載下的任務完成率、穩定通信、自主避碰、故
障處置與回收成功率。研究部認為，能累積跨季節海試資料、維修經驗及可重複交付能力的船廠將
更具競爭優勢。

從政策走向可持續交付，軍工供應鏈蓄勢 
國防預算擴張、無人載具產業政策及非紅供應鏈需求，有利支持業者持續投入研發與量產。無人系
統的產業效益亦將由載具製造延伸至感測器、通信、任務整合與反制設備，並隨部署規模增加，帶
動備件、維修、軟體更新及訓練等後續需求，為供應鏈建立持續性收入來源。 
無人機方面，長榮航太(2645)則以軍用商規艦載監偵無人機切入市場，具備航太製造與品質管理能
力的延伸優勢。漢翔(2634)透過與 Shield AI 合作的 V-BAT 垂直起降無人機取得海巡案，後續成長
機會包括在地整合、維保及新增採購；反無人機方面，創泓科技(7714)布局 C2 無人機防禦系統與 
Sentrycs 反無人機方案，可望拓展偵測、辨識、反制及指揮控制整合業務。

無人艇方面，台船(2208)的「奮進魔鬼魚」採三體船設計與模組化酬載，具備拓展多元海上任務的
潛力；龍德造船(6753)的「黑潮」則以高速操縱與匿蹤設計為特色，結合既有軍用小型船建造經驗，
爭取量產與後勤需求。研究部認為，後續隨產品驗證與採購逐步落實，具備系統整合、穩定交付及
長期維保能力的業者，將有機會將政策需求轉化為實質營運成長。

緯穎 6669 TT(電子–電腦及週邊設備) 
GPU、ASIC 並進，27 年獲利創高 
投資與評價 
1. 因缺料影響降低，加上 Trainium3 開始出貨，緯穎 3Q26 營收優於預期
17%，上修獲利 25%至 EPS 43.9 元，3Q26 營收、獲利將同創新高。 
2. 在 ASIC、GPU 貢獻下，4Q26 營收估季增高個位數 ，AI 比重將逾 55~60%，
因 2H26 優於預期，上修今年營收 8%至 1.46 兆元，EPS 140.3 元。 
3. AMD Helios 為緯穎 27 年 AI 強勁動能，加上公司為 Trainium 水冷產品主
供，帶動 27 年稅後獲利 YoY+60%，CB 對股權稀釋僅 1.03%，EPS 估續
創新高達 224.71 元，維持買進評等，目標價 2,400 元。 
重點更新 
3Q26 營收、獲利上修，反映一般伺服器雙位數季增及液冷 ASIC 加入貢獻 
緯穎 3Q26 營收 4,346 億元，QoQ+56%、YoY+63%，優於研究部預期 17%，
主要因缺料影響較預期低，加上 9 月 AWS Trainium3 開始出貨，因本季一般伺
服器占比超越 50%，估毛利率維持 8.53%，稅後獲利上修 25%至 245.95 億元，
YoY+59%至 EPS 43.9 元。預期 3Q26 營收、獲利將同創新高。  
4Q26 AI 比重攀升至 55%以上，ASIC、GPU 同步貢獻，上修今年營收 8% 
研究部估緯穎 4Q26 營收將季增高個位數，AI 部分，ASIC 出貨 Trainium3 液冷
貢獻，ASP 估較氣冷版翻倍成長；此外，GPU 部分，預期 AMD Helios 及 Oracle 
Vera Rubin 皆有少量出貨，加上缺料持續改善，估 AI 比重將超越 55~60%，使
毛利率下滑至 8.2%，稅後獲利 YoY+81%，EPS 44.5 元。因 2H26 狀況優於預
期，上修今年營收 8%至 1.46 兆元 ，YoY+54%，反映下半年 AI 比重攀升至 55%，
毛利率 8.38%，稅後獲利 YoY+54%，EPS 140.34 元。 
27 年營運在 ASIC、GPU 成長增速下，獲利將再創新高 
展望 2027 年，預期 AMD Helios 將為 AI 強勁動能，2027 年 AMD GPU 機櫃估
達五到六千櫃 ，ASP 500 萬美元 ， 加上VR 機櫃貢獻 ， 估使GPU 占比拉升至 30%。
ASIC 部分，我們認為緯穎仍是 Trainium 水冷產品主供，預期 AWS 水冷比重大
幅攀升，估AWS 營收年增四到五成；一般伺服器維持兩成以上增長。綜上，上修
27 年營收 11%至 2.64 兆元，YoY+80.4 %，因 AI 占比估達六到七成，估毛利率
下滑至 7.35%，稅後獲利 YoY+60%，EPS 224.71 元，獲利續創新高。籌資部分，
緯穎將於 11 月完成 150 億元國內可轉債發行，預計在 1Q27~2Q27 分別使用 
80 億元、70.75 億元，對於整體股權稀釋僅 1.03%，影響有限。

## Document 513: 为什么是隔夜？央行短端利率调控机制再进一步

Source PDF: all report pdf/为什么是隔夜？央行短端利率调控机制再进一步.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-10-20
Pages: 3
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

“ 隔夜利率代表市场最短端的流动性松紧状况，没有⻓端的期限⻛险⼲扰，信号最及时、最
纯粹，适合⽤来传递央⾏当前的政策⽴场。 ” 有业内⼈⼠对《⾦融时报》记者解释，隔夜市
场体量最⼤、交易最活跃，主要经济体央⾏公开市场操作⼤多盯住隔夜利率，通过调控短
端利率，再配合预期管理，由短及⻓传导，影响整条收益率曲线。
摘要：
在 2026 ⾦融街论坛年会开幕式现场，中国⼈⺠银⾏⾏⻓潘功胜表⽰，进⼀步优化短端利率调控
机制。
“ 银⾏间隔夜回购利率，即通常说的 DR001 ，作为货币市场交易活跃、最具基准性的利率，能够
灵敏反映短期资⾦供求状况，近年来运⾏也更趋稳定。为了进⼀步提⾼利率调控的精准性，⼈
⺠银⾏将研究择机把央⾏公开市场主要操作品种的期限切换为隔夜，公开市场隔夜回购利率将
相应成为主要政策利率。 ” 潘功胜表⽰。
这也符合市场此前预期。“2025 年起， DR001 取代 DR007 成为货政报告附图的代表性利率，表
明央⾏实际上已更多关注隔夜市场利率。此后在多场新闻发布会上，⼈⺠银⾏有关负责⼈多次

提到，引导隔夜利率在政策利率⽔平附近运⾏，也向市场传达了关注隔夜利率的信号。 ”有业内
专家告诉记者，今年 6 ⽉底开始增加隔夜逆回购操作品种，进⼀步提⾼对隔夜利率的调控能⼒。
这些⼯作都为本次论坛宣布以隔夜利率作为政策⽬标利率打下了坚实基础。
为何是隔夜利率？
在业内专家看来，隔夜利率是货币市场交易最活跃、具有基准性的利率。
“ 隔夜利率代表市场最短端的流动性松紧状况，没有⻓端的期限⻛险⼲扰，信号最及时、最纯
粹，适合⽤来传递央⾏当前的政策⽴场。 ” 有业内⼈⼠对《⾦融时报》记者解释，隔夜市场体量
最⼤、交易最活跃，主要经济体央⾏公开市场操作⼤多盯住隔夜利率，通过调控短端利率，再
配合预期管理，由短及⻓传导，影响整条收益率曲线。
“ 历史上⼈⺠银⾏曾主要关注 7 天利率，与市场发展、操作传统等有关。 ” 业内专家谈到， 2012 年
之后，⼈⺠银⾏开始常态化开展公开市场逆回购操作，主⼒操作品种选⽤ 7 天，这与过去⾦融机
构头⼨管理以⼀周为主有关。此后，央⾏逐步形成 “ 做 7 天、盯 7 天 ” 的模式，培育了 DR007 作为主

要观测指标。⼀⽅⾯是因为期限与公开市场 7 天品种⼀致，便于市场理解；另⼀⽅⾯也是因为当
时 7 天利率相对于隔夜更为稳定，能够较好传导货币政策信号。
“ 主要政策利率与主要操作品种的期限由 7 天切换为隔夜，其要义在于调控精准度的跃升。 ” 光⼤固
定收益⾸席分析师张旭对记者解释， 7 天公开市场操作（ OMO ）投放资⾦需在⾦融体系内停留⼀
周，⽽隔夜操作当⽇到期，即投放作⽤于哪⼀⽇，影响便⽌于哪⼀⽇，流动性管理的颗粒度由
此从 “ 周 ” 细化⾄ “ ⽇ ” 。
“‘ 进⼀步提⾼利率调控的精准性 ’ ，其机制内涵正落于此。 ” 张旭谈到。
“ 今年年初以来，央⾏已多次向外传递市场基准利率应该更加关注 DR001 这⼀隔夜品种，其成交
量占⽐常年在 90% 以上，更能反映货币市场的真实情况，对于政策⽽⾔，更短期的市场基准利
率调控难度会更低，海外⼤部分成熟央⾏都锚定隔夜品种。 ” ⼴发证券资深宏观分析师钟林楠表
⽰，⽽此前央⾏主要操作品种为 7 天逆回购， DR001 对标的政策利率为 7 天逆回购利率，在期限

上明显错配。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⽣活家 搜索

### Page 2

在钟林楠看来，将公开市场操作品种切换为隔夜、政策利率切换为隔夜逆回购利率，⼀则有助
于提⾼流动性管理的精细度，更好地 “ 削峰填⾕ ” ，提⾼政策对市场利率的掌控⼒；⼆则实现市场
利率与政策利率期限匹配，理顺政策利率传导机制。
与国际主要央⾏实践类似
放眼国际，主要经济体央⾏普遍重视隔夜利率。张旭援引国际经验谈到，⻓期限公开市场操作
占⽐偏⾼会降低央⾏应对未预期流动性冲击的灵活性，以隔夜期限开展微调操作正是成熟央⾏
保持应对弹性的通⾏安排。
有业内⼈⼠告诉记者，从国际上看，美联储以联邦基⾦利率作为政策利率，为其设定⽬标区
间，这⼀利率对应存款类机构之间的隔夜⽆担保融资交易。⽇本银⾏在 2024 年调整货币政策框
架后，以⽆担保隔夜拆借利率为政策利率。欧洲央⾏有三⼤关键利率，分别是中枢的 7 天主要再
融资操作利率，以及作为利率⾛廊的隔夜存款便利、边际贷款便利利率。不论哪种模式，盯住
的市场利率主要是隔夜期限，再通过市场交易和预期传导，影响其他期限的利率。
此外，隔夜⼯具也⼴泛⽤于各国央⾏⽇常操作。美联储每⽇开展常备回购和隔夜逆回购操作，

分别向符合条件的机构提供短期融资和资⾦存放渠道。欧洲央⾏的边际贷款便利和存款便利作
为利率⾛廊的上下限，也采⽤隔夜期限，银⾏可根据资⾦余缺使⽤。隔夜⼯具期限很短，操作
安排上有连续性，⾦融机构会更⽅便安排⽇常流动性管理。与此同时，欧洲央⾏仍保留 7 天期的
主要再融资操作和 3 个⽉、 6 个⽉、 1 年期的⻓期再融资操作，隔夜⼯具与较⻓期限⼯具并⾏是其
操作体系的重要特点。
“ 总体来看，短期市场利率围绕清晰的⽬标利率或政策利率运⾏，是主要央⾏操作框架的共同特
征。 ” 业内⼈⼠解释。
沿袭货币政策价格型框架转型思路
对于本次调整，业内专家普遍认为，此时央⾏宣布将研究择机将隔夜操作作为央⾏主要操作品
种，是货币政策制度框架进⼀步改⾰完善的延续，这⽅⾯的深化改⾰也到了具备条件的时候。
回顾近两年来，央⾏⼀直在不断完善利率调控机制。 2024 年，⼈⺠银⾏明确 7 天期逆回购操作利
率为主要政策利率，由央⾏根据货币政策调控需要确定，以此推动货币政策框架向价格型调控
转型。
同时，逐步淡化其他期限⼯具利率的政策⾊彩， 2025 年 3 ⽉，中期借贷便利（ MLF ）由单⼀价位

中标调整为多重价位中标，完全退出政策利率功能，回归中期流动性投放⼯具的基本定位。主
要政策地位的明确，可以给市场⼀个清晰的参照，减少利率⾮理性波动，让货币政策的价格信
号传得更好、传得到位。
央⾏还配套创⽴了临时正逆回购操作，稳定隔夜市场利率波动。 2024 年，隔夜临时正逆回购操
作也相应推出，利率分别是政策利率减 20bp 和加 50bp ，有利于突出政策利率地位，框住市场利
率波动。 2026 年，临时回购的操作利率区间宽度进⼀步收窄为政策利率 ±25bp ，有效增强对短
端利率的调控能⼒，更好发挥政策利率定价锚作⽤。
“ 本次央⾏提出的调整，仍属于货币政策框架改⾰的内容。央⾏的重点还是在不断完善整个制度
框架，并对近中期改⾰思路做了⽅向性的规划。 ” 有业内专家告诉记者，从刚刚公布的 9 ⽉宏观经
济数据看， GDP 增速、社会消费品零售总额、规上⼯业增加值等多项指标均好于预期，经济运
⾏总体平稳，前期宏观调控政策的效果已在逐步体现。

### Page 3

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

国信证券经济研究所所⻓助理兼银⾏业⾸席分析师王剑认为，随着隔夜操作⾛向常态化，政策
利率或从 7 天过渡⾄隔夜，但不宜和降息划等号。⼴发证券⾸席经济学家郭磊也提到，央⾏在持
续完善⾃⾝的货币政策调控框架，⼀系列制度设计是⼀脉相承的，沿袭了向价格型框架转型的
思路，市场也要避免将央⾏操作机制改⾰举措当作降息来炒作。

## Document 519: 企业套期保值效果如何评估

Source PDF: all report pdf/企业套期保值效果如何评估.pdf
Report source: 宝城期货
Date: 2026-10-19
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

投资咨询业务资格：证监许可【2011】1778 号
企业套期保值效果如何评估
宝城期货 陈栋
套期保值的核心目标是通过期现反向对冲，降低企业现货经营的价格波动风险、平滑经营收益。市场
实务中，多数企业仅以套保盈亏绝对值评判套保效果，存在明显的评价片面性与结果偏差。科学的套保效
果评价不应聚焦单一盈亏结果，而应量化评估组合风险降低水平、波动稳定性及极端风险抵御能力。本文
构建以风险降低程度、波动率优化、VaR 极端风险检验为核心的标准化套保有效性评估框架，明确各项指
标的理论内涵、计算逻辑与评价标准，形成可落地、可量化、可对比的套保效果评价体系，为企业优化套
保比率、校验套保策略、完善风险管理体系提供理论支撑与量化依据。
衡量套保有效性的三大核心维度
大宗商品价格具备高波动、强趋势、易受外部冲击的特征，实体企业通过期货套期保值对冲原材料采
购、产品销售及库存存货的价格敞口风险，是当前期现结合经营的主流模式。在套保实操中，受基差波动、
套保比例偏离、行情结构性分化、换月滑点等因素影响，完全套期保值在现实市场中几乎无法实现，多数

套保组合仅能实现部分风险对冲。长期以来，国内实体企业对套保效果的评价普遍存在误区：以期货端盈
利认定套保有效，以期货端亏损认定套保失败，忽略了套保对冲现货价格波动、稳定经营利润的本源功能。
这种单一结果导向的评价方式，会导致企业频繁调整套保策略、追逐短期收益，偏离风险管理本质。从衍
生品风控理论来看，套期保值有效性的核心定义为，套保后期现组合整体风险相较于裸敞口现货风险的下
降幅度。风险降低程度、波动率改善水平、极端风险覆盖能力，是衡量套保有效性的三大核心维度。基于
量化指标构建标准化评估框架，能够客观区分套保策略优劣，剔除主观判断偏差，实现套保效果的科学化、
常态化校验。
套期保值有效性核心评估框架构建
构建的套保有效性评估体系，以裸头寸现货资产为基准参照物，以套保后期现组合资产为评估对象，
从整体风险降幅、收益波动率、极端尾部风险三个层级完成全方位检验，形成由浅入深、由常态到极端的
完整评价体系。
风险降低程度是评判套期保值效果最核心、最通用的一级指标，直观反映套保业务对企业原有价格风

险的对冲效率，也是学界与行业通用的最优套保比率测算依据。该指标的核心逻辑是对比未套保现货收益
率方差与套保后期现组合收益率方差的差异。现货裸头寸的经营风险全部来源于价格波动，方差越大，代
表企业经营业绩波动越剧烈、经营稳定性越差。开展套期保值后，期货与现货反向对冲，组合收益率方差
会显著降低，降低幅度即为套保风险对冲效果。风险降低程度的量化公式为：套保有效性 =（现货方差 -
套保组合方差）/ 现货方差 ×100%。指标取值区间为 0 至 100%，数值越接近 100%，代表套保对冲效
果越好，价格风险剥离越彻底；数值趋近于 0，代表套保基本无风险对冲效果；若数值为负，说明套保不
仅未降低风险，反而放大了企业整体经营波动，属于无效套保、劣化套保。在企业实操评价标准中，通常
将有效性高于 70% 定义为优秀套保效果，50%–70% 为有效套保，30%–50% 为基本有效，低于 30% 为
无效套保。该指标能够客观量化套保策略的风险对冲价值，规避 “盈利即有效、亏损即无效” 的主观误
区，是套保有效性评估的核心标尺。

波动率优化评估：经营稳定性量化检验指标
收益率波动率是衡量资产价格波动剧烈程度的核心指标，直接对应企业经营业绩的稳定性，是套保有
效性的二级核心评价维度。企业风险管理的核心诉求，除降低整体风险外，更在于平滑年度、月度经营利
润波动，避免业绩大起大落，适配企业稳定经营、投融资及内控管理需求。未开展套保时，企业现货资产

### Page 2

投资咨询业务资格：证监许可【2011】1778 号
收益率波动率高，在大宗商品单边涨跌行情中，极易出现大幅盈利或大幅亏损，经营确定性极差。规范的
套期保值业务，通过反向持仓对冲，能够显著压低组合收益率波动率，让企业经营收益维持在相对平稳区
间。波动率评估框架主要包含两项对比维度：一是标准差对比，对比套保前后收益率标准差，标准差下降
幅度越大，套保平滑波动的效果越显著；二是最大回撤对比，统计区间内套保前后资产最大亏损幅度，套
保后最大回撤显著收窄，说明套保能够有效抵御阶段性单边行情冲击。相较于风险降低程度的整体量化，
波动率指标更侧重过程稳定性检验，可以精准识别结构性行情中的套保表现。例如部分套保策略整体风险
降幅达标，但阶段性波动率剧烈，说明套保比例动态调整不足，存在阶段性风控漏洞，需要优化策略节奏。
VaR 风险指标检验：极端尾部风险防控评估
传统方差、波动率指标仅能衡量常规行情下的平均风险水平，无法覆盖地缘冲突、政策突变、极端涨
跌停、隔夜跳空等小概率、大损失的尾部风险。而 VaR（风险价值）指标是衡量套保组合极端风险抵御能

力的关键指标，构成套保有效性评估的终极风控维度。VaR 的核心定义为：在一定置信水平与持有周期内，
资产组合可能面临的最大潜在亏损。本文套保评估体系采用 95%、99% 双置信水平下的 VaR 指标，对比
现货裸头寸与套保组合的极端风险差异。从实操逻辑来看，大宗商品极端行情下，基差异动、流动性枯竭、
跳空缺口等问题会导致常规套保效果弱化。通过 VaR 检验，可以精准测算极端行情下套保的风险覆盖能
力：若套保组合 VaR 数值显著低于现货 VaR，证明套保不仅能够对冲常态波动风险，还能有效抵御极端
尾部风险，风控体系具备稳健性；若套保前后 VaR 差异较小，说明该套保策略仅适配平稳行情，无法应
对极端市场冲击，属于不稳健套保策略。VaR 指标有效弥补了传统二阶矩评估模型的短板，实现了常态风
险 + 极端风险的全覆盖，让套保有效性评估更贴合真实市场风险结构。
不同指标的综合评价逻辑与应用标准
三大指标并非独立评判，而是形成互补式、层级化的综合评价体系，构建完整的套保有效性校验逻辑。
风险降低程度作为一级核心指标，用于判定套保整体是否具备避险价值，是策略存续的基础依据；波

动率指标作为过程校验指标，用于评价套保对企业经营稳定性的改善效果，优化套保节奏与持仓管理；VaR
极端风险指标作为兜底校验指标，用于检验套保策略的抗冲击能力，排查极端行情风控漏洞。在企业常态
化评估应用中，完整的有效套保需要同时满足三项条件：风险降低程度达标、组合波动率显著下降、极端
VaR 风险有效收缩。若仅单一指标达标，其余指标失效，则代表套保策略存在结构性缺陷。例如部分固定
比例套保，整体风险降幅达标，但极端 VaR 偏高，说明策略缺乏动态调整能力，无法应对突发行情；部
分套保波动率优化较好，但风险降低程度偏低，说明对冲比例不足，风险剥离不充分。基于该框架，企业
可实现月度、季度、年度套保效果量化复盘，动态修正最优套保比率，摒弃经验化、主观化的套保管理模
式，实现套期保值从 “经验交易” 向 “量化风控” 转型。
套期保值有效性评估是企业衍生品风险管理体系的关键闭环环节，科学的评价体系必须脱离单一盈亏
导向，回归风险对冲本源。本文构建的以风险降低程度为核心、波动率优化为支撑、VaR 极端风险检验为

兜底的三维评估框架，完整覆盖了常规波动与极端行情的全场景风险评价。风险降低程度量化了套保整体
避险效率，波动率指标检验了企业经营稳定性改善效果，VaR 指标补齐了尾部极端风险的评估短板，三者
结合形成标准化、可量化、可落地的套保有效性评价体系。企业依托该框架开展常态化量化评估，能够精
准识别无效套保、劣化套保，持续优化套保比例与交易策略，进一步规范期现业务管理，真正实现以期货
工具稳定现货经营、降低价格风险、平滑企业业绩的风险管理目标。
发表于 2026.10.19 期货日报第三版

◼ Fed 如預期升息 1 碼 ， 為近3 年來首次升息 ， 且點陣圖暗示年底前仍有升息空間 ，
反映 Fed 強化抗通膨立場。美國公債殖利率維持高檔震盪，會後債市雖出現底部
修復跡象，但利率與通膨變數仍在。投等債殖利率亦升至 5.7%，顯著高於 10 年
均值 3.98%，今年資金仍持續流入，反映市場鎖利需求未因利率波動而轉弱 。 
◼ 美國投等債企業 2Q 營收年增 10.5%、EBITDA 年增 15.8%，皆創近 4 年來最強
表現，主要受惠大型科技企業與 AI 投資、油價走高，以及醫療保健營收貢獻，總
槓桿率降至近 3 年低點 ， 利息保障倍數維持穩定 ， 企業營運與償債能力仍具韌性。 
◼ 不確定環境下，利率高檔震盪可能延續，存續期相對較短的金融債較具抗利率波
動優勢 。金融產業獲利亦具支撐 ，2026 年銀行 、資本市場與金融服務EPS 年增率
分別可達 23%、19%與 14%，以銀行與資本市場表現較突出，尤可關注短天期、
體質穩健，且獲利動能較明確的相關金融債發行公司。

2026 年初至今報酬率 
美國非投等債報酬率 1.93 
美國投等債報酬率 -1.02 
美國公債報酬率 -1.28

美國 10 年公債殖利率 4.93% 
西德州原油期貨 $101/桶

### Page 2

2

誠信親切、專業創新 週報 
▎高殖利率吸引鎖利，資金穩健流入 
近期美國投等債殖利率隨美國公債殖利率攀升至 5.7%，
顯著高於 10 年均值 3.98%，使收益水準回到近年相對
高檔（圖 2）。儘管今年以來美國 10 年期公債殖利率
震盪走高， 且9 月初利率上行壓力再度升高，但年初以
來國際資金仍持續流入投等債基金（圖 3）。從週度流
量觀察 ， 多數期間維持淨流入 ， 且截至9 月 9 日當週仍
延續正流入 ， 顯示利率上行與短線報酬波動尚未明顯削
弱投等債需求 ， 資金仍傾向透過投等債鎖定較高殖利率
水準。

圖 2 美國投等債殖利率升至 5.7%，高於 10 年均值

資料來源：Bloomberg，2026/9/17

圖 3 今年公債殖利率上行，但資金仍流入投等債基金

資料來源：JPM，2026/9/9

▎企業獲利仍具支撐，投等債基本面穩 
檢視美國投等債企業第 2 季基本面 ， 營收與獲利動能仍
維持穩健 。2026 年 2Q 營收年增 10.5%，EBITDA 年增
15.8%，兩者皆創 2022 年 3Q 以來、近 4 年來最強表
現 （圖4、圖 5） 。從產業來看，改善主要受惠於科技、
能源與醫療保健，其中能源受油價走高支撐，科技則由
大型科技企業與 AI 相關投資鏈帶動，推升整體獲利表
現。顯示投等債的支撐來源不只在於殖利率升高，企業
營運獲利仍維持韌性，信用基本面仍相對穩健。

圖 4  2Q 營收年增 10.5%，創 2022 年 3Q 以來最高

資料來源：JPM，2026/9/9

圖 5 EBITDA 年增 15.8%，近 4 年來最強表現

資料來源：JPM，2026/9/9

▎槓桿率下降，付息能力維持穩定 
獲利改善也反映在資產負債表指標上。今年 2Q 美國投
等債企業總槓桿率降至 3 倍，為 2023 年 4Q 以來低點
（圖 6）。在利息成本仍高的環境下，EBITDA 成長仍
支撐償債能力 ， 使利息保障倍數維持在10.3 倍 （圖7）。
% 
單位:百萬美元

整體來看，企業獲利增長不僅支撐營運表現，也帶動槓
桿率下降，高利率對信用指標的壓力仍相對可控。

圖 6 美國投等債總槓桿率改善，降至近年低點

資料來源：JPM，2026/9/9

圖 7 高利率環境，美國投等債利息保障倍數仍然持穩

資料來源：JPM，2026/9/9

▎金融獲利延續成長，金融債具韌性 
從標普金融產業來看， 銀行、 資本市場與金融服務3 類
次產業的獲利動能同樣延續。2026 年 EPS 年增率分別
達 23%、19%與 14%，其中銀行、資本市場表現最為
突出。從季度變化來看，銀行2Q EPS 年增率達 41%，
資本市場也達 28%， 上半年獲利動能明顯較強 。 雖然下
半年成長率放緩 ， 但銀行與資本市場3Q、4Q 仍維持雙
位數成長，金融服務亦延續正成長（圖 8），顯示金融
產業獲利表現仍有延續性 ， 也使金融債在投等債內部更
具觀察價值。

圖 8 銀行與資本市場 EPS 成長較突出

資料來源：Bloomberg，2026/9/16

▎利率波動未歇，可關注短天期金融債 
短線來看，Fed 偏鷹、油價高檔與通膨黏著，仍可能使
美國公債殖利率維持震盪 ， 長天期債券價格也較容易受
利率變動牽動。不過，在美國投等債殖利率位於近年高
檔、企業獲利與信用指標仍具韌性的環境下，投等債仍
具備收息鎖利與平衡股票市場波動的配置價值 。 若擔憂
短線利率波動，可側重短天期、體質較佳的金融債，以
相對較低存續期降低價格波動 ， 同時保留高殖利率環境
下的票息優勢。

## Document 545: 凱基投顧_2337 旺宏_沈漢軒_20261016

Source PDF: all report pdf/凱基投顧_2337 旺宏_沈漢軒_20261016.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-10-16
Pages: 7
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

十月 16, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R  Powered by the EFA Platform 1

動態更新 記憶體 ‧ 台灣

旺宏 
(2337.TW /2337 TT)

3Q26 法說預覽：利基需求支撐長期獲利  
重要訊息 
旺宏將於 10/27 公告 3Q26 財報並召開法說會 。 
評論及分析  
產出與庫存限制 3Q26 出貨，ASP 提升支撐毛利。3Q26 營收 242.3 億元，
達我們預估的 91.6%。儘管 NOR/SLC NAND/eMMC 報價分別季增約 40-
60%/70- 100%/20 -30%，營收僅季增 26.7%，我們推測主因為：(1) 1H26
將 NOR/ROM 產能轉向 eMMC，造成轉換期間的產出損失； (2) NOR/SLC 
NAND 庫存較低 ；(3) eMMC 良率提升不如預期。上述因素導致 NOR/SLC 
NAND 出貨量季減，eMMC 則僅小幅季增。不過，ASP 上漲仍將帶動毛利率
提升至 71.5%，EPS 預估調整至 6.1 元。 
預估 4Q26 EPS 8.2 元。展望 4Q26，我們預期：(1) SLC NAND 報價續漲
40%；(2) eMMC 漲幅收斂至個位數；(3)旺宏 NOR 報價策略將明顯較華邦
電(2344 TT ，NT$161.5 ，增加持股 )保守，主因產能與庫存較為受限。位元
出貨方面，隨 TLC/MLC 良率持續改善，eMMC 出貨量可望季增 15%；惟
SLC NAND/ NOR 受限於庫存有限、產能未提升，出貨量將大致持平。我們預
估 4Q26 營收達 292.3 億元，季增 20.6%，毛利率提升至 75.6%，修正 EPS
預估至 8.2 元。 
利基需求支撐價格地板，長期獲利具延續性 。展望 2027-28 年，受惠 Edge 
AI 裝置、Boot Drive 及航太國防等重視可靠度的利基應用需求穩健，我們預
期 SLC NAND/NOR 供需仍將緊俏，報價維持高檔；eMMC 則以車用需求最
為穩定。若設備順利進駐，旺宏預計於 4Q27 新增 2.1k 3D NAND 與 4k 12
吋 NOR 產能，並於 1H28 貢獻產出，主要為 支應上述應用需求。我們將
2027 年營收預估修正至 1,446.5 億元，毛利率 77.9%，EPS 42.8 元。儘管
受限於漲幅放緩與產能有限， 短期獲利增速不如原先預期，考量上述利基應
用將支撐 Flash 價格地板，我們仍看好旺宏長期獲利的延續性。  
投資建議  
考量獲利成長低於原先預期，我們將目標本益比下修至 5 倍(相較利基型記
憶體龍頭南亞科(2408 TT ，NT$447.5，增加持股)的 7 倍)，以 2026-27 年
EPS 為評價基礎，目標價 155 元。惟長期獲利仍穩健，且目前評價具吸引
力，維持旺宏「增加持股」評等。  
投資風險  
擴產設備進機時辰延後 、工廠營運與人力面臨瓶頸。  
增加持股‧維持 
  收盤價 October 16 (NT$)   107.0

延續性。

## Document 550: 凱基投顧_Bank of America Corporation BAC_施志鴻_20261014

Source PDF: all report pdf/凱基投顧_Bank of America Corporation BAC_施志鴻_20261014.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-10-14
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

十月 15, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R  Powered by the EFA Platform 1

動態更新  金融‧美國

Bank of America Corporation 
(BAC.N/BAC.US)

3Q26獲利受一次性罰款影響; 2027F核心獲利維持動能 
重要訊息 
美國銀行 (BAC) 3Q26 每股盈餘 0.91 元，低於市場預估 17%，若扣除反洗錢
罰款 12億美元(每股盈餘 0.17元)，則低於市場預估 3%。 
評論及分析 
3Q26 獲利衰退受一次性罰款及資本市場 波動影響。BAC 3Q26 稅後淨利僅
69.3 億美元，季減 24%、年減 17%，若扣除 12 億美元之一次性反洗錢監
管罰款，調整後淨利為 81 億美元，季減 10%、年減 2%，主要非利息收入
季減 13%(投資收益季減 44%、手續費收入季減 3%)，反映 3Q26 資本市場
波動，抵銷核心淨利息收入(NII)季增 3%。3Q26 淨呆帳提存季減 7%，抵銷
營業費用季增 6%(扣除罰款則季減 7%)。1-3Q26 營運槓桿因一次性罰款則
轉為負向 0.45 百分點 ，若扣除一次性費用則仍為正 1.8 百分點。  
3Q26 淨利差持續上揚、資產品質持續改善 。BAC 3Q26 淨利差季增 8 基點
至 2.16%，主要來自 3Q26 放款年增 6%、季增 1% (主要美國商業放款及
信用卡各年增 9%及 5%)、低收益資產到期 重新訂價，帶動存放利差擴大 5
基點。3Q26 總存款年增 2%、季增 1%，資金成本因存款結構持續優化( 非
計息存款年增  5%)，季增幅僅 提升 3 基點至  1.49%。資產品質 亦持續改
善，3Q26 呆帳沖銷率季減 3 基點至 0.44%，主要信用卡呆帳沖銷率季 減
33 基點至 3.22%，企金沖銷率季減 1 基點，帶動 3Q26 逾放比季減 0.03%
至 0.44%。BAC 3Q26 普通股權益比率(CET1)季減 35 基點至 10.9%，其中
AOCI 之債券部位虧損 18 億元(影響約 10 基點)，但資本返還超過獲利，配
發股利及實施庫藏股共 83 億元，高於主要同業。  
2027F 淨利息收入展望正向，營運槓桿 回升至 200-300 基點。管理層重申
2026F 淨利息收入達年增 8%目標， 並預期 2027 淨利息收入年增超過
8%(約增加 51 億美元)，主要反映非計息存款成長、放款成長、固定利率重
定價及 3 次升息 25 基點之效益，中期淨利差目標維持 2.3%。在營運效率
方面，公司預期 4Q26 回到正向營運槓桿，2027F 營運槓桿目標 200-300 基
點。針對市場關注的監管缺失，管理層表示已取得重大改善進展，並強調本
次同意令與富國銀行過往情況不同，並未設有資產規模上限。公司對 2027F
資產品質有信心，指出目前未見轉弱跡象。 至於 AI 授信與風險控管政策，
BAC 強調嚴格評估借款人的現金流集中度、 企業現金流。  
投資建議 
美國銀行雖一次性罰款影響今年獲利約 4%，但核心營運動能穩定，隨著存
放款結構持續優化，公司預估 2027F 淨利息收入年增率超過 8%，加上資產
品質良好，2027F 營運槓桿回升至 200-300 基點，公司維持穩定股利配發及
買回庫藏股，維持美國銀行「增加持股」評等 ，目標價 67 元，約 2026F -
27F 平均每股盈餘 14 倍(過去五年本益比區間為 8-16 倍)。 
投資風險 
通膨造成美國聯準會再度升息；美國 消費支出趨緩；全球金融市場轉弱 。 
增加持股 ‧ 維持

### Page 5

美國 Bank of America Corporation

十月 15, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R 5

圖 14：公司概況  圖 15：BAC 組織架構圖 
美國銀行 (Bank of America Corporation) 創立於 1784 年，總
部位於美國北卡羅來納州夏洛特，為美國第二大銀行。截至
2Q25 總資產規模為 3.44 兆美元，員工總數約 21.2 萬人。公
司透過八大事業體營運，涵蓋消費金融、優選銀行、商業銀
行、企業與投資銀行、財富管理與全球市場業務，客戶遍及全
球逾 30 個國家，服務近 6,900 萬名個人與中小企業客戶。 
美國銀行積極發展數位化與國際布局，強調資本效率與風險控
管，並透過穩定配息與股份回購持續強化股東回饋，品牌形象
聚焦穩健經營、數位創新與永續金融發展。

### Page 7

美國 Bank of America Corporation

十月 15, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R 7

項目 定義 
能源使用 
直接與間接能源消耗總量 (十億焦耳 ) 
- 公司運營範圍內消耗之能源總量 
- 能源使用總量  = 直接能源消耗總量  + 間接能源消耗量 
- 總能源使用量包括購買的能源、生產的能源 
- 就公用事業而言，輸電 /電網損耗為其業務活動之一部分，被視為總能源消耗，數據不包括為滿足能源使用而生產的電力
（公用事業為出售而生產） 
- 就公用事業而言，用於能源生產的煤、天然氣或核能等原料不屬於「總能源使用」項下  
購買之再生能源 
Primary 再生能源購買總量 (十億焦耳 ) 
- 公司各種來源之能源消耗量與購買的能源中屬於自然界可再生者（太陽能、風能、水能、生物質能、地熱能）之量   
- 如無證據顯示再生能源由公司生產，則所報告的能源數據視為購買的再生能源  
再生能源使用率  再生能源占總能源消耗量之比例  
CO2 約當排放量 
直接 CO2 與 CO2 約當排放量 (公噸) 
- 公司擁有或控制的來源的直接排放量（範圍  1 排放量） 
- 相關氣體：二氧化碳  (CO2)、甲烷  (CH4)、一氧化二氮  (N2O)、氫氟碳化物  (HFCS)、全氟化合物  (PFCS)、六氟化硫  (SF6)、三氟
化氮 (NF3)  
CO2 約當排放量營收比 
直接 CO2 與 CO2 約當排放量（公噸）銷售（百萬元）比 
- 公司擁有或控制的來源的直接排放量（範圍  1 排放量） 
- 相關氣體：二氧化碳  (CO2)、甲烷  (CH4)、一氧化二氮  (N2O)、氫氟碳化物  (HFCS)、全氟化合物  (PFCS)、六氟化硫  (SF6)、三氟
化氮 (NF3)  
廢棄物總量 
廢棄物總量 (公噸) 
- 總廢棄物  = 無害廢棄物  + 有害廢棄物 
- 僅考慮固體廢棄物，但如液體廢棄物以公噸為單位呈報，則會將其加入求得包含液體廢棄物之總量 
- 對於採礦、石油與天然氣等行業， tailings 、廢石、煤、飛灰等廢棄物亦考慮在內  
廢棄物回收率 
公司呈報的廢棄物回收率 
- 廢棄物回收率  = 廢棄物回收量 /總廢棄物 *100 
- 廢棄物轉化為能源或經由廢棄物焚燒產生能源視為廢棄物回收 
- 經由堆肥回收的廢棄物視為回收之廢棄物  
總取水量 
總取水量 (立方公尺 ) 
- 由呈報組織直接或經水公司等中介機構從任何水源抽取的總水量 
- 水井、城鎮 /公用事業 /市政用水、河水、地表水等不同之水源均予以考慮  
環保支出 環保支出總金額 - 所有用於環境保護，或防止、減少、控制環境因素、影響、危害的投資與支出，亦包括處置、處理、衛生、清理支出  
員工流動率 
員工流動率 
- 包括任何原因（自願或非自願）離開公司的員工，如辭職、退休、自然離職 /死亡、醫療失能、冗員、裁員、重組、解僱、
裁減或定期合約到期 
- 員工流動率  =（離開之員工 /平均員工人數） *100 
- 平均員工人數  =（本年末員工人數 + 去年末員工人數） /2 
- 本財年末員工數  =上個財年末員工數  + 新員工數  - 離開之員工數  
女性經理 
女性經理百分比 
- 女性經理占公司經理的百分比 
- 如有不同階層之百分比，如最高階、高階、中階、初階，則會考慮中階女性經理之百分比 
- 女性經理百分比  = 女性經理人數 /經理總數 *100  
女性員工 
女性員工百分比 
- 女性員工占公司員工總數的百分比 
- 女性員工百分比  = 女性人數 /員工總數 *100  
教育訓練總時數 
所有員工教育訓練總時數 
- 僅考慮員工教育訓練時數 
- 包括一般員工所有類型的教育訓練（如健康與安全、環境、急難事件救援、技能與職業發展） 
- 如果數據以天為單位，則乘以  8，係假設  1 天 =  8 小時工作  
每位員工教育訓練時數  平均每年每位員工總教育訓練時數  
股東治理分數  衡量公司用以反收購工具的有效性  
公司治理分數  衡量公司對最佳治理原則的承諾和有效性  
產品責任分數  衡量公司生產優質產品和服務的能力，且產品是否將客戶的健康、安全、整合性和數據隱私進行綜合考量  
社區關係分數  衡量公司對成為優良公民、保護公眾健康和尊重商業道德的承諾  
勞動力分數  衡量公司在員工工作滿意度、健康、工作場所的安全、多樣性、平等以及員工發展機會方面的成效  
資源使用指標  衡量公司在原物料、能源或水的使用效率，以及是否通過改進供應鏈來尋求更俱生態效率的解決方案

## Document 558: 凱基投顧_Takeaway_9938 百和_劉昃恩_20261014

Source PDF: all report pdf/凱基投顧_Takeaway_9938 百和_劉昃恩_20261014.pdf

Pages: 3

### Page 1

十月 14, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R  Powered by the EFA Platform 1

法說重點摘要

百和 
(9938 TT, NT$39.45, 持有)

重點摘要  
1. 3Q26 自結每股盈 餘 0.61 元，年減 46%、但季增 27%，主因 客戶
下單模式 轉變及原物料成本攀升等因素大幅侵蝕 旺季利潤率 。 
2. 目前終端需求偏保守，客戶以急短單模式下單逐漸常態化 。 
3. 初步規劃 2027 年擴建越南新廠，並購置印尼新土地。 為因應客戶
產能分散 需求， 未來將進一步新增印度供應據點 。 
結論 
油價強勢 仍將壓抑終端需求與鞋 服供應鏈利潤率展望 。凱基 目前 給予
百和「持有」評等及目標價 36.8 元。

3Q26 財報 
 3Q26 自結稅後淨利 1.8 億元，折合 每股盈餘 0.61 元，年減 46%、
但季增 27%。儘管營收 回升至 44.1 億元，年增 6%、季增 16%，
但客戶下單模式轉變及原物料成本攀升 等因素導致毛利率 季減 0.2 個
百分點至 29.5% ，年減擴大至 6.3 個百分點； 營利率僅 9.9% ，年減
7.9 個百分點 ，創下 1Q24 以來新低 。 
 1Q-3Q26 產品別 營收佔比  (不含房地產 )：織帶/鞋帶 42.9%、針織經
編緹花 19.9%、鬆緊帶 13.4%、黏扣帶 12.7%、射出勾 7.0%、其
他 4.1%。 
 受到產品組合不利影響， 1Q-3Q26 經編網布營 收年減 5%。 
 3Q26/ 1Q-3Q26 梭織緹花營收 分別為 1,200 萬/ 5,600 萬元，佔比
0.4% / 0.5% 。 
 3Q26 產品別毛利率 ：織帶/鞋帶 30.4%、經編網布 17.2%、鬆緊帶
37.9%、黏扣帶 27.6%、射出勾 44.0%、梭織緹花 32.4% 、其他
37.5%；合併 29.5%。 
 1Q-3Q26 產品別毛利率：織帶 /鞋帶 30.2%、經編網布 19.9%、鬆
緊帶 37.7%、黏扣帶 27.6%、射出勾 44.2%、梭織緹花 44.4% 、
其他 42.3%；合併 30.3%。 
 1Q-3Q26 客戶別營收佔比： Adidas ( 德) 25.1%、Nike ( 美) 15.7%、
On ( 瑞士) 6.1%、New Balance ( 美) 4.4%、Asics (日) 3.3%、Under 
Armour ( 美) 2.9%、Columbia ( 美) 2.5%、Hoka ( 美) 2.3%、BoA 
(美) 1. 6%、Lululemon ( 加) 1. 6%；前十大佔比 65.5%，對 比 1Q-
3Q25 為 67.3% 。 
 1Q-3Q26 客戶別營收成長率： Adidas 年減 5%、Nike 年增 2%、On
年減 8%、New Balance 年減 11%、Asics 年增 3%、Under Armour
年減 1%、Columbi a 年增 9%、Hoka 年增 58%、BoA 年增 24%、
Lululemon 年減 6%。

 1Q-3Q26 客戶別 鞋類營收成長率： Adidas 年減 6%、Nike 年持平。 
 1Q-3Q26 客 戶 別服飾 營 收 成 長 率 ：Adidas 年減 3% 、Nike 年 增
11%。 
 1Q-3Q26 應用別營收佔比：鞋 類 60.4% 、成衣 25.3%、3C 2.8%。 
 3Q26/ 1Q -3Q26 應用別營收成長率：成衣年增 27%/ 年增 12%、鞋
類年持平 / 年減 1%、3C 年增 20%/ 年增 16%、醫療年增 31%/ 年

 1Q-3Q26 集團合併 資本支出 22.7 億元，百和及百和興業 KY 分別佔
3.3 億、19.4 億元，建築物 、設備及其他 則分別佔 8.6 億、13.7 億、
0.3 億元。百和/ 百和興業 KY 個體部分，建築物、設備及其他分別佔
0.5 億/ 8.2 億、2.8 億/ 11.0 億、0.1 億元/ 0.2 億元。

營運展望 更新 
 目前終端需求偏保守， 客戶以急短單 模式下單 逐漸常態化 。 
 公司開發創新產品能力優於同業， 有利 在兩大國 際品牌客戶 中保持
較高滲透率 。 
 G 牌智慧型手錶錶帶 銷量暢旺，未交訂單追加至 300 萬條，遠高於
原估需求 30 萬條/年。 
 近年 3C 產品使用百和材料佔比已高達 80%，跳脫二十年前 僅賺取
加工費模式 ，附加價值提升 有利於毛利率 增加。 
 預計 2026 年資本支出 約 28 億元 (不變)；其中，百和及百和興 業 KY
分別佔 4.4 億、23.7 億元。 
 初步規劃 2027 年擴建越 南新廠，並購置印尼新土地。未來將 進一步
新增印度供應 據點。 
 截至 9 月，公司擁有 353 項各國專利，另申請中專利尚有 逾 50 個。

## Document 581: 周小川：应当开展研究并做好预案，应对人工智能相关投资失败可能带来的金融风险

Source PDF: all report pdf/周小川：应当开展研究并做好预案，应对人工智能相关投资失败可能带来的金融风险.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-10-20
Pages: 2
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。
周⼩川指出，技术演进⽆疑会导致部分投资失败、部分投资成功的局⾯，但考虑到如今有
巨额的资⾦流向⼈⼯智能及相关基础设施，⼀旦其中⼀部分失败，就可能引发⾦融动荡，
并酿成危机，这就需要采取⼤规模的⾏动来⽀撑经济，并对部分⾦融机构实施救助。因
此，应当开展研究并做好预案，在政策⼯具箱中储备相应的政策⼯具，以应对这类动荡和
影响。
摘要：
中国⼈⺠银⾏原⾏⻓周⼩川 10 ⽉ 20 ⽇在 2026 外滩年会 “AI 与⽣产率⾰命：技术跃迁下的宏观冲
击 ” 圆桌环节上表⽰，对⽣产率提升来说，最主要是来⾃ AI 应⽤层。他也表⽰，要为⼈⼯智能相
关领域投资失败可能带来的⾦融⻛险做好应对预案。
“ ⾦融⾏业正在投资 AI 相关领域。 AI 相关领域⼤致有三个类别。⼀类是⼤模型，以及⽤于训练的
算⼒和数据中⼼及其他。⼆是 AI 应⽤领域，这也是中国更加关注的领域。三是⼀些为 AI 提供⽀持

的⾏业，包括半导体、电⼒、液冷等等。我们认为投资于这些是安全的，因为⽆论 AI 如何发展都
需要⽔和电。 ” 周⼩川指出。
由于 AI 对全球经济和社会结构的巨⼤影响，⼤家都呼吁在国际层⾯就这个问题进⾏合作。
“ 但因为 AI 变化太快，以⾄于政府部⻔间建⽴ AI 治理框架⾯临着很⼤的难度。但我觉得⾄少有三
件事可以做。⼀是控制军事上的⽤途，⼆是打击⿊客和欺诈者，三是要保护个⼈的隐私，这与
数据使⽤等等有关。其他⽅⾯，⽬前仍处于观察和研究阶段。例如，已有与会者谈到 AI 对就业结
构、收⼊分配和社会结构的影响。但我认为，⽬前尚未形成共识，我们可以继续观察。 ” 周⼩川表
⽰。
他强调，伴随 AI 发展，全球供需关系平衡、产业结构都会发⽣巨⼤变化。作为⾦融业的从业者和
学者，应着⼒让价格机制发挥重要作⽤，以此应对全球平衡的变化。因此，或许我们不应过于
依赖⾏政⼿段来防范相关影响，⽽是应让价格调整来反映正在发⽣的结构变化，以及我们应采
取何种应对⽅式以适应新的结构。
他同时指出，对于技术演进，我们⽆疑会⾯临部分投资失败、部分投资成功的局⾯，但考虑到

如今有如此巨额的资⾦流向⼈⼯智能及相关基础设施，⼀旦其中⼀部分失败，就可能引发⾦融
动荡，并酿成危机，这就需要采取⼤规模的⾏动来⽀撑经济，并对部分⾦融机构实施救助。
“ 因此，我们应当开展研究并做好预案，在政策⼯具箱中储备相应的政策⼯具，以应对这类动荡
和影响。 ” 周⼩川表⽰。
本⽂来源：澎湃新闻

## Document 616: 宏观经济深度研究：承压的韧性与加速的分化

Source PDF: all report pdf/宏观经济深度研究：承压的韧性与加速的分化.pdf
Report source: 工银国际证券
Date: 2026-10-20
Pages: 5
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

宏观经济深度研究 - 2026 年 10 月 15 日 1 
证券研究 
宏观经济深度研究 
承压的韧性与加速的分化

“疾风知劲草，烈火见真金。 ”2026 年以来，中东冲突持续，能源价格波动，贸
易摩擦加剧，融资成本抬升，全球经济受到地缘政治、供给冲击与金融条件收
紧的多重考验。但尽管置身于多重外部冲击交织的复杂环境中，全球经济仍展
现出了超预期的韧性。IMF 于 10 月 13 日发布最新《世界经济展望》报告，将
2026 年全球经济增速预期小幅上调 0.1 个百分点至 3.1%，2027 年维持 3.4%不
变。然而，总量稳定的背后，一场更加深刻的结构变化正在发生：全球经济的
韧性正在由共同承压走向结构分化。面对共同的冲击，不同经济体的增长表现
既取决于自身的缓冲空间，也取决于对新一轮技术周期的承接能力。具体来
看，三重趋势值得关注：第一，冲击正在被吸收而非消失，供给弹性、能源替
代、企业适应和政策缓冲共同支撑全球经济韧性。第二，总量稳定掩盖增长重
构，能源禀赋与科技禀赋成为决定经济体分化的重要变量。第三，人工智能正
在从需求扩张进入生产率验证阶段，科技投资虽为当前增长提供重要支撑，但
其能否进一步转化为持续的供给改善仍待观察。与此同时，连续冲击不断消耗
财政与政策缓冲，使得政策空间持续收窄。但这并非意味着政策可以松懈，在
AI 红利尚待兑现、传统风险仍未退潮的换挡阶段，若能提高有限财政资源的使
用效率，将有助于缓解关键供给约束并支持长期生产率提升。 
价升量韧：全球经济韧性超预期。 尽管中东局势持续干扰，能源价格带动通胀
上行，全球经济增长预期仍然保持相对稳定。从总量来看，虽然 2026 年全球经
济并未摆脱放缓压力，IMF 本次更新预计全球经济增速将由 2025 年的 3.5%回落
至 2026 年的 3.1%，并预计于 2027 年重新升至 3.4%。但值得注意的是，相较 7
月预测，IMF 将 2026 年全球经济增速小幅上调 0.1 个百分点至 3.1%，2027 年维
持 3.4%不变，显示多重冲击下全球经济展现出超预期的韧性。这一变化与价格
预测形成鲜明反差， IMF 预计 2026 年全球通胀率将由 2025 年的 4.1%升至
4.7%，并上调 2027 年通胀预测 0.2 个百分点。这一“价升量韧”的组合，反映出
本轮外部冲击的传导特征与此前预期有所不同。传统负向供给冲击通常表现为
“价升量降”，即能源和原材料成本上升一方面推高通胀，另一方面通过压缩居
民实际收入、侵蚀企业利润并收紧金融条件拖累实际产出。而当前的供给冲击
更多地表现为价格压力和政策约束，对实际经济活动的拖累则被部分吸收，背
后主要有三方面原因： 首先，全球经济对传统化石能源的直接依赖程度较过去
下降，能源效率提高、供应来源更加多元，使单位能源冲击对应的产出损失有
所降低。其次，企业和供应链经过地缘冲突和贸易摩擦的连续冲击后，调整能
力明显增强，库存管理、供应商替代和运输路线重构提高了经济体系的缓冲空
间。最后，AI 及相关硬件、算力和数据中心投资形成了新的需求支撑，对部分
能源冲击造成的拖累形成对冲。 
共振异频：总量稳定结构分化。相比全球经济总量的稳定，本次 IMF 展望中更
加显著的变化来自经济增长结构预期的调整。 一是空间上的分化。 按照本次预
测，32 个经济体贡献了全球 95%的增长，而今年 4 月达到同一比例需要 54 个经
济体。这意味着，全球总量增长虽然保持相对稳定，但增长动能正在向更少数
经济体集中，增长基础有所收窄。从各经济体表现来看，美国 2026 年和 2027
年经济增速预计均为 2.3%，其中 2027 年增速预期较 7 月上调 0.1 个百分点；德
国 2026 年增速预计为 1.3%，较 7 月大幅上调 0.6 个百分点；英国 2026 年增速
同样预计为 1.3%，较 7 月上调 0.3 个百分点。与之相对的，能源冲击和地缘风
险暴露更高的经济体增长明显承压，中东和中亚地区 2026 年增速预测较 7 月下
调 1.0 个百分点至-0.3%，其中沙特阿拉伯更是大幅下调 5.0 个百分点至-3.3%。
二是时间上的错位。IMF 预计 2026 年发达经济体增速达到 2.1%，较 7 月预测上

证券研究 
调 0.4 个百分点；新兴市场和发展中经济体增速预计为 3.7%，较 7 月下调 0.1 个
百分点。而到了 2027 年，发达经济体增速预计回落至 1.8%，与 7 月预测持平；
新兴市场和发展中经济体则重新加速至 4.4%，虽较 7 月预测仍小幅下调 0.1 个
百分点。由此来看，2026 年的增长改善更多集中于发达经济体，而新兴市场的
相对优势则更多体现在 2027 年的增速水平上。能源禀赋和科技禀赋或是决定这
一轮分化的两个重要变量： 一方面，中东冲突导致全球能源价格上升，能源进
口经济体面临进口成本、通胀和实际收入的多重压力。 另一方面，人工智能投
资周期快速扩张，使处于半导体、数据中心、算力基础设施以及相关技术价值
链上的经济体获得额外需求支撑。两种力量交叠，使全球经济事实上形成新的
增长坐标：既能够减少能源冲击、又能充分参与科技周期的经济体表现最为稳
健；而既高度依赖能源进口、又较少参与技术价值链的经济体则面临更大的增
长压力。 
图 1: 人工智能准备度与能源结构

资料来源：IMF 以及工银国际 
注：该图根据 IMF 的 AI 准备度指数以及 2024 年非化石燃料能源占总能源供给的比重对各经济体进行绘制。非化
石燃料能源包括可再生能源和核能。

科技面临换挡时刻，政策更需精准托底。当前 AI 首先体现为一轮资本开支驱动
的正向需求冲击，基础设施投资快速扩张，并通过贸易与投资链条为全球增长
提供支撑。但从中长期来看，决定 AI 能否抬升全球增长中枢的关键仍是技术创
新能否进一步转化为生产率提升。IMF 指出，未来 AI 能否通过生产组织重构、
知识创造和劳动力配置改善形成持续的供给扩张，仍有待验证。 2026 年正处于
AI 由投入向产出转换的重要阶段。一方面，科技投资已经成为全球经济抵御能
源和地缘冲击的重要支撑。另一方面，如果生产率红利未能及时兑现，快速扩
张的资本开支也可能面临盈利回报下降、资产价格重估和金融条件收紧的风
险。也正因如此，当前经济虽仍有韧性，并不意味着宏观政策可以松懈。过去
数年，各经济体连续通过财政补贴、税收减免和其他措施吸收能源与地缘冲击
带来的影响，反复调用政策缓冲使财政空间逐步收窄。因此，未来宏观政策更
需要在增长仍保持稳定时修复财政空间、强化能源和供应链安全，并提高社会
保障体系对弱势群体的覆盖能力，这意味着政策方式需要更加精准。 IMF 研究
显示，收入支持型政策更能托底需求和消费，但会带来一定通胀压力；价格压
低型政策更能迅速压低表面通胀，但财政代价更高，而且对消费的支撑更弱。
在生活成本冲击下，若通过普遍价格干预实现与定向收入支持相同程度的家庭
保护，前者所需财政资源可能达到后者的 3 至 22 倍。因此，在 AI 红利尚待兑

### Page 3

宏观经济深度研究 - 2026 年 10 月 15 日 3 
证券研究 
现、传统风险仍未退潮的换挡阶段，若能提高有限财政资源的使用效率，则有
利于更精准地保护弱势群体、缓解关键供给约束并支持长期生产率提升。

科技股依舊是配置核心
投
資
策
略
市場展望投資策略
 投等債布局風險相對小：十年美債殖利率已逐步反應
FED升息預期，過去經驗，在此利差水準(十年殖利率與
政策利率利差)布局，風險承擔相對較小。然而，債務問
題依舊是短期利率可能上升的壓力。(詳見Page 13)
 持續買進台股：台股持續創下新高，台積電10/15召開
3Q26法說，高機率繼續釋放樂觀前景，推升後續上漲
動能，同步助益台股前景，4Q26台股有望繼續成為領
漲全球股市的關鍵。(詳見Page 17)
 產業布局鎖定大型科技股 ：AI趨勢明確，唯一的不確定
性源自利率風險是否再次攀升 ，衝擊股市估值 。Mag7、
半導體巨頭成長性高且估值普遍相對便宜 ，相對其餘產
業類股更能抵禦利率衝擊。(詳見Page 15)
 匯市展望：升息預期疊加歐洲債務疑慮支撐美元強勢 ，
然而隨著美德利差短期飆升過快 ，先前漲勢兇猛的美元
或暫時歇息 ，維持高檔盤整 。然而，法國政局不確定性
恐一路拖至 2027年6月，中長期資金仍可能持續從歐洲

流入美國 ， 支撐美元長期走勢 。 本週美元指數區間
100.5~103。
 債市展望：儘管法國、美國、日本政治官員相繼釋出試
圖穩定市場情緒的財政計畫或談話 ，但市場反應明顯不
買單，願景與口號終究無法安撫投資人信心 ，殖利率短
期 仍 有 續 攀 升 風 險， 本 週 美 債 十 年 殖 利 率 區 間 看 在
5.2%~5.4%。
 股市展望  : 3Q26財報季將至 ，市場預估 S&P500整體
EPS年增率高達 24.5%，強 勁 的 盈 餘成 長 依 舊是 支撐
4Q26股市創高關鍵 ，惟短期仍須留意利率攀升對於市
場廣度的衝擊。本週標普500指數區間7,650~7,950。

1. 10/4巴西總統首輪選舉前總統之子 弗拉維奧 ·波索納羅

西股、債、匯市狂歡慶祝 ，雙方將於 10/25進行第二輪
決選。(詳見Page 6)
2. 法國極右派候選人勒龐 (Le Pen)提出每年削減 250億歐
元的政府支出計畫，目標是5年節省1,250億歐元，預計
至2030年將公共赤字/GDP的比例降低於3%。該計畫主
要針對移民政策 、歐盟繳交預算款項 、退休金改革、減
稅等項目進行。
3. 高市早苗承諾將控制公債發行規模 ，並迅速因應市場動
盪，即便政府提高支出以提振經濟成長 ，仍將奉行財政
永續性原則，包括檢討現有租稅優惠與補貼 。美國財長
貝森特認為 ，GDP增 長持續超 過 3%有助於 壓 低債務
/GDP比重。
產業訊息
1. Marvell於投資人日上調2028FY營收預期至200億美元，
同時首度釋出2031FY營收目標700~900億美元。公司認

為AI潛在市場規模至2030年有望達到4,000億美元，主要
由Google、微軟、亞馬遜等超大規模雲端服務商自研客
製化晶片帶動。(詳見Page 5)
2. Google再度擴大核能布局 ，與Constellation  Energy分
別簽署20年購電協議 (特定核電機組升級 )和15年能源供
應協議(不綁定單一電廠)。兩項協議涉及電力規模合計達
3,590MW，激勵Constellation  Energy和核能股價上漲。
3. 東芝宣布投資 600億日圓 (3.8億美元 )擴建HDD產能，衝
擊希捷 、威騰股價 。然而，大摩估計東芝擴產年化增速
30%，在市占相對較小的情況下 ，對整體供給影響不大 ，
同時與加速增長的需求相比仍有缺口。

CAGR增速高達 58.7%，高於2027FY預計的 47%，代

度高，達成機率大。

業趨勢背書 ，科技股在前景明確下 ，依舊會是投資主
軸和資金青睞的方向。

巴西大選變天，股市有望複製歷史
6
波索納羅首輪總統大選位居領先 圖表解讀
➢ 10/4巴西舉行首輪總統大選 ，前總統之子弗拉維奧·波
索納羅意外以 47.1%的得票率領先現任總統魯拉 ，雙
方將於10/25進行第二輪決選。此次波索納羅的領先明
顯超出市場預期 ，在選前的平均民調上 ，波索納羅落
後2.3%，也因此在首輪取得領先後 ，巴西股市 、債市
預期新政府較容易推動財政緊縮 、企業減稅等政策而
大漲慶祝，
➢ 2010~2022年巴西四次總統大選後股市普遍走跌(90個
交易日) ，唯一上漲是2018年(雅伊爾.波索納羅勝選)，
同樣主張縮減政府開支 ，加上當時通膨降溫 ，引導巴
西殖利率走低 。目前巴西正經歷同樣的市況 ，市場預
期未來1年巴西央行仍有7碼降息空間。
➢ 目前MSCI巴西股市預期PE為9.4X，不僅低於20年平均
的10.4X，也低於過去四次總統大選前的水位 ，為後續
股市創造上漲空間。

通膨有望持穩，官員談話或淡化升息預期
7
重點提醒
➢ 本週全球總經重點聚焦在通膨數據 ，以及FED官員談話
態度，在美債殖利率持續飆升之際 ，成為市場短期定價
的風向球。
➢ 8月美國核心 PCE維持3%的穩定增速 ，再次證明美國並
未見到通膨上升的壓力 ， 同時 9 月即時通膨指標
Truflation平均年增率 2.41%，也同樣低於 8月的2.51%，
然而通膨僵固性確實存在。9月FED剛展開升息循環，利
率上升對於物價的壓抑效果尚未體現 ，預期9月CPI仍將
維持穩定增速。
➢ Hammack 9月談話明顯鷹派 ，認為企業與家庭 或將高
物價視為常態 ，使高通膨更難消除 ，政策須維持限制性
立場。而Waller 9月談話相對鴿派 ，認為若通膨未明顯
攀升，仍傾向維持利率不變 ，在期貨市場預期未來 FED
仍有4碼的升息空間情況下，或有機會降溫市場情緒。

美元續強，G7釋儲油壓抑油價

匯市表現說明
➢ 歐元： 歐洲央行官員表態 ，法國債券走勢目前尚不足以
觸發其重啟購債計畫門檻 ，同時法國財政部表示將維持
發債策略，歐元仍承受極大壓力。
➢ 瑞朗： 相較於全球主要央行重啟升息循環 ，瑞士央行持
續將政策利率維持在 0%，在美元走強之際 ，同步引導瑞
朗走弱。
➢ 原油： 沙國正持續透過替代管道將原由運出 ，中東原油
出口的恢復，疊加G7宣布在4個月內釋出最多一億桶柴油、
原油儲備，引導油價下跌。
➢ 黃金：美元升值疊加殖利率持續上漲 ，8月以來持續抑制
金價走勢 。然而，全球央行買入黃金的趨勢並未縮減 ，
中國人行連續 23個月增持黃金 ，長期依舊看好金價動能 。

### Page 9

法國財政見不到曙光，美元維持強勢預期
9
短期美德利差或引導美元暫歇 圖表解讀
➢ 法國財政疑慮引發歐元走弱 ，同時疊加美國升息 、債
務持續性問題 ，美德利差快速飆升 ，推升美元指數走
強。然而，短期美德 10年利差上升速度過快 ，加上殖
利率已完全反應FED升息預期，美元指數升勢或獲得暫
緩空間。
➢ 然而，中長期仍看好美元維持相對強勢 。2027年4月
18號~5月2號法國將舉行總統大選 ，預期新任總統將
於6月再次解散國會改選以解決國會僵局 。但以目前賭
盤機率 ，極右派 Le Pen和中間派 Philippe分別以 33%
和26%位居前二 ，未有黨派能單獨過半的情勢仍高機
率延續至2027年。
➢ 在市場無法見到明確的政治僵局突破或財政赤字緩解
的跡象前，不確定因素將延續至 2027年，持續干擾歐
元，引導美元強勢。
